    1-185. The XO and commander organized the staff in Table I-IV. In consultation with the commander,
    the XO uses this table as a last look for staff organization and operations. The rehearsal will give the XO
    time to confirm or reorganize the staff as needed based on manning and mission requirements. If changes
    are required, the XO makes those changes before the Table VI certification. The principal staff officers
    go through the same review process and make recommendations to the XO. During this table, the
    commander identifies, and the XO defines all additional duties yet to be assigned. See Individual Tasks
    150-LDR-5013, Organize the Staff for Operations; 150-C2-0000, Organize the Command and Control
    System as the Staff Officer; and Collective Task 71-BN-5123, Task Organize for Operations.

ESTABLISH THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

    1-186. This task is performed in unison with establishing the CP. The S-3 designates a battle captain and
    a COP manager prior to or during this event. The COP manager works for the battle captain. The COP
    manager is a person selected and designated to serve in this position due to their high degree of attention
    to detail, C2IS experience, computer skills, knowledge of FM 6-0, intelligence expertise, and experience
    working in a CP. The COP manager’s duties include:

         • Displaying all information required for the COP and decision-making.
         • Updating unit locations and events through the digital component of the COP.
         • Updating unit locations and events on the analog COP.
         • Coordinating with other staff sections for COP manipulation during briefings.
         • Receiving guidance from the command group on specific views or manipulations of the COP

              for situational understanding purposes.
         • Coordinating COP requirements with higher headquarters and subordinate units.

    1-187. Both the COP manager and the battle captain work under the authority of the S-3 and are part of
    the KM team. The C2 information integrator assists in the establishment of the COP as well. C2

42                                                       TC 6-0.2                                        30 July 2025


---

                                        Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Battalions

     information integrators are the commander's subject matter experts regarding operation, maintenance,
     integration, and operator training for unit C2IS. The C2 information integrator duties include:

          • Integrating CP C2 information systems.
          • Assisting in establishing the CP network.
          • Troubleshooting C2 information systems architecture.
          • Assisting the COP manager in developing the COP.
          • Implementing a unit individual digital training program.

     1-188. While the C2 information integrator and the S-6 establish the framework and systems for the
     COP, the population of that framework and integration into training is the responsibility of operators
     within the staff, under the guidance of the COP manager and KMO. The XO ensures that the staff
     establishes the COP to meet the requirements of the mission, in accordance with unit SOP and the
     commander’s intent.

     1-189. COP displays within a CP are conspicuous, recognizable, and accessible to those attempting to
     gain and maintain situational awareness. Adequate COP visibility sets the condition for effective battle
     tracking and decision-making. CP personnel prioritize and emplace COP displays—both digital and
     analog—to facilitate situational awareness. See Collective Tasks 71-BN-5319, Establish the Common
     Operational Picture, and Individual Task 150-C2-5315, Establish the Common Operational Picture.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

     1-190. The XO trains the staff to achieve a go in all performance measures on the T&EO for Collective
     Task 71-BN-5100, Conduct the Operations Process for Command and Control. The staff will conduct
     the operations process fully supporting the commander and the mission. Each section will maintain
     running estimates and recommend any plan variations and changes in a timely manner to the commander.
     The staff will conduct the operations process while conducting tasks from the battalion’s standard
     METL, A-METL, or other tasks assigned by the higher headquarters or the commander.

PREPARE AN OPERATION ORDER

     1-191. The battalion is required to publish an OPORD as part of the operations process. The staff must
     be proficient in conducting the MDMP, and the OPORD reveals that proficiency. The length and detail
     of the order can vary due to time and the mission. They also distribute the order to higher, adjacent,
     lower/subordinate, supporting, and supported units. The XO evaluates the OPORD against the basic
     standards established in Collective Tasks 71-BN-5119, Prepare an Operation Order.

REACT TO A FRAGMENTARY ORDER

     1-192. At some point during the operation, the commander (or XO acting in their behalf for training
     purposes) initiates either a verbal or a written FRAGORD. This can be for any reason as determined by
     the commander and following the training scenario. For example, the battalion commander has the staff
     react to a FRAGORD from brigade directing a transition of the force temporarily into a hasty defensive
     posture. The staff will conduct RDSP to ascertain necessary changes to the current plan and recommend
     changes to the commander within the designated timeline. See Collective Task 71-BN-5203, Process a
     Fragmentary Order, and 71-BN-5002, Conduct Rapid Decision-Making and Synchronization Process.

FINALIZE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

     1-193. The XO and principal staff officers will use the commander’s guidance and the staff rehearsal to
     finalize the battle rhythm in the unit SOP. The XO will ensure that the commander has adequate time to
     participate (exercising command presence) during the operations process, and ample time for battlefield
     circulation and key leader engagements. This battle rhythm, at a minimum, will ensure all inputs and
     outputs are based on resources, the DSM/DST, mission, training objectives, delegated authorities, and
     the commander’s intent directly in support of decision-making and resource allocation. Refer to
     Collective Task 71-BN-5125, Implement the Battle Rhythm.

30 July 2025  TC 6-0.2  43


---

Chapter 1

FINALIZE DECISION SUPPORT PRODUCTS

     1-194. The DSM is an integral part of staff priorities. Here the staff refines the DSM and DST as well
     as the processes for employing and updating them. The DSM is tied to both the operations process and
     is a product of the MDMP. The DSM and DST portray key decisions and potential actions that are likely
     to arise during the execution of each COA (FM 5-0, Ch. 5). The DSM is tied to resources, events, and
     decisions that the commander needs to make affecting outcomes. During the event, the staff will brief
     the commander on any changes to the DSM and DST. Delegation authorities are stipulated within the
     DSM and DST for decisions that must be made in the commander’s absence. Refer to Individual Task
     150-C2-5138, Develop a Decision Support Template, and 150-C2-5139, Develop a Decision Support
     Matrix.

EMPLOY THE COMMAND AND CONTROL SYSTEM

     1-195. The XO fine tunes the C2 system, which includes:
          • Personnel – Ensures the staff is organized and all personnel are positioned appropriately
                according to job skill and the needs of the command.
          • Processes– Ensures all lessons learned to this point in the tables are captured in the unit SOP.
                All inputs and outputs from the various cells, boards, working groups, and planning teams are
                updated. Information management and its display are within command guidelines.
          • Networks – Ensures the servers, wires, networks and C2IS are updated, operating, and secure.
          • CPs – Ensures all equipment is maintained, reserved, and ground cleared. All command
                equipment (including vehicles) is maintained and prepared to execute the mission.

     1-196. Changes identified for retraining or adjusting during the Table V rehearsal are made prior to the
     Table VI certification. Refer to Individual Task 150-LDR-5251, Organize the Command and Control
     System.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-197. The staff personnel conduct CP operations to support the commander in developing a shared
     understanding and making decisions. They execute several routine and recurring tasks to establish,
     operate, and move the CP. With the assistance of the staff, commanders develop processes and
     procedures for the execution of these tasks and capture them in the CP SOP. See ATP 6-0.5 and
     Collective Task 71-BN-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

CONTROL OPERATIONS

     1-198. While executing one of the battalion METs the staff assists the commander in controlling unit
     operations by arranging forces in time, space, and purpose to produce maximum relative combat power
     at the decisive point. During preparation phase of the operation, the staff monitors the progress of
     subordinate units’ conduct of precombat checks and inspections (PCIs), mission-specific training,
     implementation of risk controls, preparations to sustain themselves, and protecting themselves. Once the
     operation begins, the staff sections:

          • Advise and inform the commander.
          • Maintain running estimates.
          • Provide recommendations.
          • Prepare plans, orders, and other staff products.
          • Assess the progress of operations against what was planned.
          • Manage information within their area of expertise.
          • Identify and analyze problems.
          • Perform risk management.
          • Exercise staff supervision over their area of expertise.

     1-199. The XO should use Collective Task 71-BN-5103, Control Operations, to evaluate the staff.

CONDUCT SECTION/CELL AFTER ACTION REVIEWS

     1-200. Staff section and cell leaders conduct internal AARs to identify adjustments to make to the SOP
     and retraining required prior to conducting certification in Table VI. Refer to 150-C2-5133, Conduct a
     Formal After Action Review for additional information.

TABLE VI: CONDUCT STAFF CERTIFICATION

     1-201. This table is conducted during a CPX or FTX (see the unit CATS). As budget and training days
     allow, this table is ideally conducted in a field environment with 24 hour operations. Certification is an
     internally evaluated event. The externally evaluated event is Table X.

     1-202. Commanders choose which tactical tasks are to be performed. The commander certifies that the
     staff have met all the training criteria for their Table VI by achieving at least a P rating in each of the
     tasks listed here. Once the staff is certified, they can proceed to unit training in Tables VII through X.

     1-203. This table is a culmination of all previous tables, and the commander can use any of the collective
     tasks from previous tables for retraining as necessary. Any retraining required from poor performance
     of tasks in the earlier tables should occur prior to Table VI.

     1-204. Commanders organize their C2 system to support decision-making and facilitate
     communications. The XO manages the C2 system—the arrangement of people, processes, networks, and
     CPs that enable commanders to conduct operations.

     1-205. The outcome of Tables I through VI is that the staff work as a cohesive unit, led by the
     commander. Each staff section/cell in each CP understands and operates within their designed
     capabilities and the mission. Certification indicates individual and section/cell competency meets Army
     standards and the commander’s intent. The staff performs the operations process seamlessly, without
     outside augmentation. Figure 1-16 on page 44 establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during
     this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

1-206. The staff must successfully complete all performance measures listed under Collective Task 71-
BN-5100, Conduct the Operations Process for Command and Control. The staff will conduct the
operations process fully supporting the commander and the mission. Each section will maintain running
estimates and recommend any plan variations and changes in a timely manner to the commander. The

30 July 2025                                              TC 6-0.2                                                                  45


---

Chapter 1

     staff will conduct the operations process while conducting tasks off their standard METL, A-METL, or
     other tasks assigned by the higher headquarters or the commander.

EXECUTE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

     1-207. The XO will ensure that the established battle rhythm is executed in support of the mission and
     the commander’s intent. See Collective Task 71-BN-5125, Implement the Battle Rhythm, and Individual
     Task 150-LDR-5011, Establish Boards and Working Groups.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-208. CP personnel execute several routine and recurring tasks to establish, operate, and displace the
     CP. With staff assistance, commanders develop processes and procedures for executing these tasks and
     capturing them in the CP SOP. See ATP 6-0.5 and Collective Task 71-BN-5200, Conduct Command
     Post Operations.

CREATE SHARED UNDERSTANDING

     1-209. The XO and principal staff officers will use running estimates, MCIS, and meetings to establish
     shared understanding across the staff. This shared understanding encapsulates the commander’s vision,
     guidance, intent, desired end state, and the operational environment. The shared understanding is
     displayed in the common operational picture by the unit mission command system. Staff members use
     Individual Task 150-LDR-5007, Create a Shared Understanding, as a reference for this task

CONTROL OPERATIONS

     1-210. The staff controls operations by arranging forces in time, space, and purpose to produce
     maximum relative combat power at the decisive point. They use tools such as staff estimates, the COP,
     DSM and DST, operations schedule, execution matrix, and graphic control measures to constantly
     evaluate progress of the operation against what was planned to occur. They take actions within their
     authority and area of expertise to bring combat power to bear and seek guidance from or make
     recommendations to the commander or higher headquarters when a situation exceeds the staff’s
     authority. The staff should use Collective Task 71-BN- 5103, Control Operations.

TRANSFER CONTROL OF OPERATIONS BETWEEN COMMAND POSTS

     1-211. The staff will pass control of the operation from one CP to another during the execution of Table
     VI, either to displace a CP or in reaction to an attack on a CP. While doing so, the commander and XO
     will ensure that they maintain continuity of C2. The staff in each CP will successfully transfer control of
     the functions for which they are primarily responsible from their CP to another CP per unit SOP, and
     then resume control when capability is restored. The XO works with the SGM on which systems to shut
     down, and in what order to ensure C2 is maintained throughout the process. The XO will use Collective
     Tasks 71-BN-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control, and 71-BN-5202, Conduct Transfer
     Control of Operations.

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

     1-212. The unit will displace each CP at least once during this training event. Critical aspects of C2, such
     as contact with higher headquarters and subordinate units, must be maintained during displacement.
     Displacements are planned to ensure the main CP is stationary during critical phases of the operation.
     All other CPs will displace per unit SOP at least once during the certification event (e.g. TAC, combat
     trains, and field trains). Refer to Collective Task 71-BN-5201, Displace the Command Post, and 71-BN-
     5205, Conduct Quartering Party Operations.

FOLLOWING CERTIFICATION

     1-213. The unit will displace each CP at least once during this training event. Critical aspects of C2, such
     as contact with higher headquarters and subordinate units, must be maintained during displacement.
     Displacements are planned to ensure the main CP is stationary during critical phases of the operation.
     All other CPs will displace per unit SOP at least once during the certification event (e.g. TAC, combat

TABLE VII: INTEGRATE THE COMMAND AND CONTROL
SYSTEM

     1-214. Table VII begins with receipt of an order from higher headquarters for the unit’s external
     evaluation. Depending on the unit type and the nature of the operation planned for the EXEVAL, the
     unit may begin receiving liaisons and building the larger team with subordinate units and unified action
     partners. The XO, under the direction of the commander, will lead the command through the MDMP
     with the inclusion of the liaisons and attachments. Cells and sections will also identify and establish
     reporting requirements through the correct channels, and the XO will ensure that the headquarters can
     report these requirements to higher. The command will task organize based on mission requirements.
     The venue and location is determined by the commander in accordance with the unit CATS and mission
     requirements. For example, the battalion commander may hold a CPX and combines tables VII through
     IX based on an abbreviated timeline for an upcoming mission. Figure 1-17 establishes the inputs, tasks

    1-216. Once the liaisons and attachments are received and integrated, the XO will lead the staff through

     Process, 71-BN-5009, Develop a Decision Support Template, and 150-C2-5139, Develop a Decision

COORDINATE EFFORTS WITH HIGHER, LOWER, ADJACENT, AND SUPPORTING UNITS

     1-217. As part of conducting the scenario driven MDMP, the XO will ensure that the cells and sections
     are properly leveraging and tasking higher, lower, adjacent, and supporting units as appropriate. The XO
     will further ensure that the battle captains and cell leads are relaying all information as a command to
     higher, lower, adjacent, supported, and supporting units based on mission requirements (understanding
     that the relaying of information may be notional for training purposes). The XO further directs all
     relevant information is shared throughout the staff. See Individual Task 150-C2-5134, Establish Liaison,
     and Collective Task 71-BN-5121, Establish Coordination and Liaison.

ASSESS THE TACTICAL SITUATION

     1-218. The commander approved an assessment plan in the Commander Table VII. The staff integrates
     that assessment plan with the training objectives, the commander’s DSM, DST, and the desired end state.
     The XO ensures all CPs contribute to assessment. The XO informs the commander of any overlaps,
     conflicts, or issues. The XO and principal staff officers use Collective Task 71-BN-5130, Assess the
     Tactical Situation and Operation.

MANAGE COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEMS

     1-219. The battalion manages C2ISs to support reliable, timely information flow in support of operations
     in the battalion AO, within the authorities granted it by unit type and/or mission. The command plans,
     engineers, installs, operates, maintains, and protects Department of Defense Information Network –
     Army (DODIN-A). The command supports operations by executing enterprise management, network
     assurance, and content management, as directed. Use Individual Task 150-LDR-5253, Manage
     Command and Control Information Systems, as a guide.

CONDUCT KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

     1-220. The commander ensures that the staff are correctly conducting KM and information management.
     The staff, led by the KMO, revise the KMP. The KM working group assesses new information
     requirements for any attachments and other partners, and updates the KMP leading to the Validation
     Exercise in Table X. All CPs/staff sections contribute to developing the KMP. See Collective Task 71-
     BN-5330, Conduct Knowledge Management.

TABLE VIII: SYNCHRONIZE OPERATIONS

     1-221. The XO synchronizes WFFs. The table is event and scenario driven in a manner that the unit can
     work towards MET proficiency with collective task T&EOs as determined by the commander. The venue
     and facility location are determined by the commander in accordance with the unit CATS and mission
     requirements. If the training scenario for Table X EXEVAL calls for the battalion to work closely with
     unified action partners, the battalion staff will incorporate partner operations and information
     requirements into the battalion’s plans, orders, and procedures during this table. The staff should practice
     the full array of battle rhythm reports and events during the execution of Table VIII. Figure 1-18 below
     establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

EXERCISE BATTLE RHYTHM

1-222. The battle rhythm is based on unit capabilities, mission requirements, and the commander’s
intent. The XO will ensure these means of controlling operations account for any attachments or
augmentation the unit will have when executing Table X. Now that the staff is working with other live
units instead of simulating their interactions, the XO may have to adjust the battle rhythm to meet the
needs of higher, lower, supported, and supporting units. Commands build upon the battle rhythm
developed in tables IV through VI. Refer to Collective Task 71-BN-5125, Implement the Battle Rhythm.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

1-223. With all attachments, liaisons, and supporting units participating in the exercise, the commander
leads the staff through the operations process. The XO will use Collective Task 71-BN-5100, Conduct
the Operations Process for Command and Control, to assess their proficiency in conducting the
operations process in connection with the collective tasks being performed for the training event. The
staff should also execute the battle drills developed prior to Table VI when conducting the Execute phase
of the operations process.

CONTROL OPERATIONS

1-224. The staff assists the commander in C2 operations by arranging forces in time, space, and purpose
to produce maximum relative combat power at the decisive point. They take actions within their authority
and area of expertise to bring combat power to bear and seek guidance from or make recommendations
to the commander or higher headquarters when a situation exceeds the staff’s authority. They use such
tools as staff estimates, the COP, DSM and DST, operations schedule, event matrix, and graphic control
measures to constantly evaluate progress of the operation against what was planned to occur. The staff
should use Collective Task 71-BN-5103, Control Operations.

CONDUCT AN OPERATIONS SYNCHRONIZATION MEETING

1-225. The current operations staff should conduct multiple repetitions of the operations synchronization
meeting and conduct an AAR after each one with the primary goal of making the meetings both effective
and efficient. Identify information that was presented that was unnecessary and coach participants to
brief their information as succinctly as they can while covering what is relevant. See Collective Task 71-
BN-5135, Conduct an Operations Synchronization Meeting.

PROCESS RELEVANT INFORMATION

1-226. Relevant information is information that commanders need to make decisions or that staff officers
need to perform their duties. Relevant information includes not just the information requirements
identified during planning, but also exceptional information that wasn’t understood to be relevant during
planning but becomes relevant during execution of operations. The staff will display and share relevant
information based on security and mission requirements and disseminates it to other units or partners to
whom it will also be relevant. The display is tied to the COP and nested with the commander’s DSM. If
unified action partners are present, the command will ensure security and foreign disclosure policies and
procedures are met. See Collective Task 71-BN-5315, Process Relevant Information.

CONDUCT REHEARSALS

1-227. Conducting the operations process includes the XO leading the staff to conduct a combined arms
rehearsal. The rehearsal nests all warfighting functions and the capabilities of the unit, attachments,
available support, or unified action partners for the mission. Staff leaders will conduct functional
rehearsals (for example, the sustainment rehearsal) in accordance with commander’s guidance and the

     battalion SOP. The commander’s DSM and the plan are updated based on the outcomes of the rehearsals.
     See Collective Task 71-BN-5122, Perform a Rehearsal.

                                                  Validation Exercise

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

    1-236. The staff conducts the operations process while the unit performs tasks on their unit standard
    METL, or other tasks as directed by the commander. The XO and staff will use Collective Tasks 71-BN-
    5100, Conduct the Operations Process for Command and Control, and 71-BN-5002, Conduct Rapid
    Decision-Making and Synchronization Process, to evaluate the staff.

CONDUCT KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

    1-237. The XO and principal staff officers will use the COP, running estimates, C2IS, and meetings to
    maintain shared understanding across the force. The KMO will lead the KM working group to update
    and implement the battalion KMP as changes to the situation drive changes in information requirements.
    Shared understanding is based on the commander’s vision, guidance, intent, desired end state, and the
    OE. Refer to Collective Task 71-BN-5330, Conduct Knowledge Management.

REVISE THE PLAN

    1-238. The staff will use the battle rhythm and the DST and DSM to make and implement execution
    decisions during the course of Table X. If variances are detected, then the staff will use RDSP to
    recommend adjustment decisions and re-synchronize the operation. The XO will ensure that the working
    groups and meetings have outcomes that directly support decisions that the command must make to
    achieve the desired end state. The meetings outlined above are nested within the unit battle rhythm and
    support higher commander’s decision-making. Refer to Collective Tasks 71-BN-5124, Revise the Plan,
    and 71-BN-5002, Conduct Rapid Decision-Making Process.

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

    1-239. The unit is required to displace each CP at least once during this training event. While doing so,
    the commander and XO will ensure that they maintain continuity of C2. The staff will successfully
    transfer C2 from the main CP to the alternate CP, and then back to the main CP upon completion of the
    displacement. Similarly, the other CPs will hand off responsibilities, move, reestablish the CP, and
    resume responsibilities. The battle captain and operations SGM direct the staff on when to relinquish

52                                              TC 6-0.2                                   30 July 2025


---

                                        Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Battalions

     responsibilities to counterparts in another CP, and to begin preparation for movement. The staff will use
     Collective Tasks 71-BN-5201, Displace the Command Post, 71-BN-5202, Conduct Transfer Control of
     Operations, and 71-BN-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-240. The staff execute routine and recurring tasks to establish, operate, and move the CPs in
     accordance with the unit SOP to ensure support to the commander’s decision-making. The staff executes
     SOP-based battle drills as exercise events trigger them (for example, the main CP receives incoming
     artillery fire). See ATP 6-0.5 and Collective Task 71-BN-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

EXECUTE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

     1-241. The XO ensures the staff execute the established battle rhythm and adjusts the battle rhythm as
     exercise events interfere with its execution. Refer Collective Task 71-BN-5125, Implement the Battle
     Rhythm.

     1-242. The outcome of Tables VII through X is external validation that the staff in each CP, with external
     augmenters and liaisons, is prepared to support the battalion commander’s decision-making and
     execution of C2 during the conduct of LSCO. Each staff section/cell and CP understands and operates
     according to their assigned roles and responsibilities. The staff performs the operations process
     seamlessly. Training shortfalls were identified by Table IX and corrected before Table X.

     1-243. Completing Table X indicates that the command is prepared to conduct METL tasks at the T
     proficiency level and is prepared at an advanced level to conduct operations. The outcome of Table X is
     that the evaluating commander has validated that the battalion staff is prepared to support the battalion
     commander in the execution of commanding forces and controlling operations during operations.

SECTION IV – BATTALION COMMAND POST TRAINING TABLES

     1-244. Section IV discusses the command and control training tables for the battalion command posts.

COMMAND AND CONTROL TRAINING TABLES FOR THE
BATTALION COMMAND POST

     1-245. The battalion employs a main CP. Depending on the unit type, the battalion may have a TAC CP,
     a combat or field trains CP, or some other alternate CP. Commanders will ensure that all battalion CPs
     are trained in accordance with unit manning and design capabilities. This training includes that the
     personnel in each CP understand the roles and responsibilities of all CP, how the CPs interact with each
     other to support the operations process, and how each interacts with higher, lower, adjacent, supported,
     and supporting headquarters.

     1-246. For the purpose of this TC, the CP consists of the junior officers and NCOs on the staff who
     conduct the routine functions that keep the CPs functioning during 24-hour operations. This section
     walks the commander, XO, battle captains and operations SGMs through the tables needed to train,
     certify, and validate CPs. This section does not cover every aspect of training, nor will it pinpoint exactly
     how one trains each collective or individual task. Commanders and leaders wanting more information
     on training the force will reference the T&EOs for each task and FM 7-0 for assistance in conducting
     training management.

     1-247. The XO and principal staff officers ensure each section NCOIC devotes a portion of their section
     training to CP operations under the guidance of the battle captains and operations SGMs. Commanders
     empower the NCOs in the battalion to complete the tasks within these tables.

     1-248. For this TC, the operations SGM is the senior NCO in each CP responsible for monitoring and
     supervising the performance of the enlisted operations staff. At the battalion level, most operations
     SGMs may be below the rank of E-9. The operations SGM assists the S-3 in all matters pertaining to the
     operations process to include planning, rehearsals, and monitoring and controlling operations in the

30 July 2025  TC 6-0.2  53


---

Chapter 1

     combined operation and intelligence cell (COIC). In addition, the operations SGM assists the CP officer
     in charge with CP administration to include the following:

          • Establishing the CP.
          • Displacing the CP.
          • Providing CP security.
          • Maintaining continuity of CP operations.
          • Executing sleep plans.
          • Managing stress within the CP.

     1-249. The operations SGM in each CP directs section NCOs to manage guard rosters, sleep plans, and
     shift schedules as well as to maintain discipline in and around the CP. The operations SGM works hand
     in hand with the headquarters company NCO to fulfill the logistics requirements, tactical employment,
     and security of the CP.

     1-250. Battalion CP Tables I through VI are directed at the individuals, teams and cells led by the
     operations SGM. The culmination of this training comes in Table V, rehearsal, and Table VI,
     certification, as the headquarters comes together to work as a collective unit. There are no external
     attachments required for the execution of these tables. The XO is the authority, in coordination with the
     battle captain and operations SGM, to transition the staff from CP Tables I through V. Tables I-II are
     designed to occur on a continuous basis as new personnel arrive to the unit. The XO and operations
     SGMs will verify training proficiency of the CP in Table V, the rehearsal for certification.

     1-251. The commander certifies each CP at Table VI allowing them to transition to the more advanced
     battalion CP Tables VII through X.

     1-252. Tables VII through X build on basic CP competency and may (depending on unit type and
     mission) require inclusion of augmentees, unified action partners, emplacement and displacement,
     operational security, and continuous 24-hour operations.

TABLE I: CONDUCT PRE-COMBAT INSPECTION (EQUIPMENT
ACCOUNTABILITY & MAINTENANCE)

     1-253. This table is the introduction to training CPs. Section leaders can use maintenance and sergeant’s
     time training to fill basic CP job skill deficiencies. For example, the operations SGM and intelligence
     NCOIC run a generator operations class for Soldiers of the CP during sergeant’s time. In Table I the CP
     leaders develop initial CP training through basic understanding of duties and responsibilities, under the
     supervision of the XO and operations SGM. Figure 1-21on page 53 establishes the inputs, tasks to be
     accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

INVENTORY COMMAND POST EQUIPMENT

     1-254. Here all members of the CP familiarize themselves on section equipment. Staff section leaders
     will train their Soldiers using individual and section collective tasks as defined by the appropriate
     proponent. For example, the S-6 NCOIC conducts an inventory of all the equipment their section is
     responsible for in accordance with the unit MTO&E. Leaders take note of what equipment is required to
     run each CP (node) and where there are gaps. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command
     Post Operations.

VERIFY COMMAND POST EQUIPMENT READINESS

     1-255. The section leader ensures that all members of the section understand how their assigned
     equipment operates both internally to their section and within the CP as a whole. Section NCOICs verify
     the readiness of their equipment in accordance with the equipment preventive maintenance checks and
     services (PMCS) and other equipment services found within each piece of equipment’s technical manual
     (TM). Each section NCOIC verifies all assigned equipment according to the TM and report any shortages
     per unit SOP. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

IDENTIFY SUSTAINMENT REQUIREMENTS FOR THE COMMAND POSTS

     1-256. The operations SGMs work with staff section NCOICs to develop CP sustainment requirements
     for current and future operations. Each operations SGM works with the headquarters company to fulfill
     those requirements in accordance with Collective Task 71-CO-0433, Support Command Posts.

SCHEDULE MAINTENANCE REQUIREMENTS

     1-257. The Section NCOICs will, based on operational requirements, manning constraints, and guidance
     from the headquarters company commander, schedule PMCS activities on a routine basis. Refer to
     Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

TABLE II: IDENTIFY COMMAND POST CHARACTERISTICS

     1-258. The section NCOICs and operations SGMs continue to supervise the CP Soldiers as they refine
     the basic and intermediate skills of their sections. Section NCOICs have primary responsibility for this
     table. Commanders staff, equip, and organize CPs to control operations for extended periods of time. CP
     personnel use information systems and equipment to support 24-hour operations. As such, CP personnel
     and equipment must be protected and sustained. This requires an effective SOP and personnel trained on
     CP administration to include the following:

          • Establishing the CP.

IDENTIFY COMMAND POST REQUIREMENTS

     1-260. Once the commander establishes the C2IS requirements (see Commander Training Table I), the
     operations SGMs work with the S-6, battle captains, and the XO to conceptualize how the CP will be
     organized and set up. This step of the tables is meant as a tabletop drill or sketch only. The operations
     SGM will lead the discussion as the other attendees provide inputs. The operations SGM works with the
     CP systems and other resources the command has on hand. If new or replacement systems are needed,
     the operations SGM brings these requests to the XO. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5202, Develop a
     Command Post Layout.

     1-261. At this point, the commander will have directed which additional CPs are to be established for
     upcoming mission requirements. At the conclusion of Table II, each operations SGM will have a detailed
     configuration of all CP requirements.

DESIGN COMMAND POST CONFIGURATION

     1-262. Commanders validate the layout of their CPs during exercises and training events, including
     positioning of personnel, information systems and displays, equipment, network cabling, and electricity
     requirements. Even as there are a number of factors to consider in laying out the floor plan of a CP,
     designs must incorporate considerations for mobility and agility to be survivable. Once the operations
     SGMs and XO get approval from the commander on their conceptualized plan for each CP, the section
     NCOICs configure their individual sections based on mission requirements. The operations SGM will
     oversee this action ensuring that system and hardware power requirements are being met. From this point
     through Table IX the operations SGMs will ensure layouts are tested, adjusted as required, and captured
     in the unit’s SOP.

     1-263. Well-designed CPs integrate command and staff efforts. Within a CP, the location of CP cells
     and staff elements are arranged to facilitate efficient internal communication and coordination. Refer to

56                                                          TC 6-0.2                                30 July 2025


---

                                        Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Battalions

     Individual Task 150-C2-5202, Develop the Command Post Layout, and ATP 6-0.5. This arrangement
     may change over the course of operations as the situation changes. Other layout considerations include:

          • Staff integration and crosstalk.
          • Trafficability.
          • COP visibility
          • Lighting and climate control.
          • Ease and speed of displacement.

REVIEW COMMAND POST STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURE

     1-264. Section leaders and NCOICs will review and update their section standard operating procedures
     (SOPs). These SOPs are nested with that of the CP as a whole. Section leaders will ensure that SOPs are
     reviewed and updated as the mission, equipment fielding, commander’s intent, and orders from higher
     headquarters dictate. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

IDENTIFY FUNCTIONAL GAPS AND COMMERCIAL SOLUTIONS

     1-265. As the design for the CP begins to take shape, and the mission and needs of the commander are
     weighed, the operations SGM will identify parts and equipment that the unit will need to obtain. Refer
     to Individual Task 150-C2-5202, Develop a Command Post Layout.

TABLE III: ESTABLISH COMMAND POST LAYOUT

     1-266. Table III begins with the command receiving a mission, whether it be an upcoming CPX, a CTC
     rotation, or another training event. This table begins configuration and functionality of future mission
     requirements into each CP (such as main, TAC, combat trains, and field trains). This table is performed
     at home station with the operations SGMs leading the effort under the direction of the XO. It is
     understood that CP and C2IS requirements are fluid at this point in the tables. However, the operations
     SGMs and section NCOICs can still get a general idea of the infrastructure requirements based on the
     commander’s decisions and guidance in Tables I through III. Figure 1-23 below establishes the inputs,
     tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

KM Knowledge Management                                       T&OE Training Evaluation Outline

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

    1-267. The XO will inform the operations SGMs and section and cell leaders of the mission and related
    IM and KM requirements. These systems enable the provision of relevant information to the right Soldier
    at the right time and in a usable format in order to facilitate understanding and inform decision-making,
    regardless of in which CP the Soldier works.

    1-268. The KM and signal personnel in CPs assist the commander and other staff members in developing
    and implementing effective KM and IM plans. Detailed doctrine on assessing, designing, developing,
    piloting, and implementing knowledge management solutions is in ATP 6-01.1. That ATP also includes
    doctrine on developing a KMP and offers techniques for content management. The operations SGMs
    will oversee the sections configuring their systems and equipment to conform to those information
    requirements. Refer to Collective Tasks 71-BN-5330, Conduct Knowledge Management, and 71-BN-
    5310, Manage Information and Data.

CONDUCT INFORMATION PROTECTION

    1-269. The battalion implements directed information protection measures using operations security
    (OPSEC), information assurance (IA), computer network defense (CND) and electromagnetic protection
    (EP) to safeguard and defend friendly information and information systems. The battalion protects
    information regardless of media - telephonic, on paper, on digital devices, or traversing networks and
    residing on information systems; from time collected until the operation is over, the information is
    archived for historical purposes, and the information is authorized to be removed from unit digital and
    analog systems. See Collective Tasks 71-BN-5010, Protect Information, and 150-C2-6111, Employ
    Operations Security Control Measures.

INTEGRATE FUNCTIONALITY

    1-270. The majority of staff work occurs within the functional and integrating cells. Staff members must
    also integrate their efforts with other CP cells and staff sections and with organizations external to the
    headquarters. Effective staff integration occurs when functional expertise from across the staff comes
    together in support of the commander’s decision requirements. The operations SGMs will lead the
    section and cell NCOICs to recommend a CP layout. For example, the operations SGM relocates the
    sustainment cell closer to the intelligence cell based on mission reports of enemy intent to target all

58                                                            TC 6-0.2                          30 July 2025


---

                  Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Battalions

     logistics routes across the area of operations. The XO then directs a mutual workspace for
     meetings/briefings. Refer to Individual Task 150-LDR-5251, Organize the Command and Control
     System.

DETERMINE COMMAND POST ERGONOMICS

     1-271. Ergonomics is defined as people's efficiency in their working environment. Each section leader
     and section NCOIC will ensure that there is adequate working efficiency in the space provided. The
     operations SGM works with sections to help locate, re-locate, or co-locate equipment and personnel that
     may need to be adjusted based on efficiency and the integration for functionality mentioned above. Refer
     to Individual Task, 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations, and 150-C2-5202, Develop a
     Command Post Layout.

IDENTIFY POWER GENERATION REQUIREMENTS

     1-272. The operations SGMs, with the assistance of the section NCOICs, identify all power requirements
     within the CP. The Operations SGM compares these requirements to power generation availability,
     keeping in mind redundancy, fueling, and maintenance requirements of systems, and then establishes a
     power generation plan for the CP that is both tailorable and portable. The operations SGM also ensures
     section personnel and mechanics understand their responsibilities with power generation. Refer to
     Individual Task, 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

     1-273. If there is a staff engineer available at the brigade headquarters, consult that subject matter expert
     when determining these requirements.

Chapter 1

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST

     1-275. As part of the event for Table IV, the commander directs the establishment of the CPs in
     accordance with established unit SOP, the commander’s intent, and operational variables. For example,
     the battalion establishes their main CP at the beginning of an STX. See Collective Task 71-BN-0050,
     Establish a Command Post.

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEM
CONSTRUCT

     1-276. As the headquarters prepares for Table V, the commander describes the purposes of the C2IS
     construct for each CP based on mission requirements and the commander’s intent. CP leaders configure
     each CP based on the commander’s guidance, the mission, and current SOPs when refining locations for
     the C2IS within the CP. See Individual Task 150-LDR-5253, Manage Command and Control
     Information Systems.

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST SECURITY PLAN

     1-277. With the CPs operational, the commander directs the establishment of CP security. The
     headquarters company leadership and the operations SGMs, under the guidance of the XO and protection
     cell, review all operational considerations and establish security plans for each CP based on mission
     variables (METT-TC(I)). The team will use Collective Task 19-CO-2204, Conduct Security of Critical
     Sites.

ESTABLISH A SUSTAINMENT SECURITY PLAN

     1-278. With the CPs operational, the headquarters company commander directs the establishment of CP
     sustainment. The operations SGMs, under the guidance of the XO, will work with the headquarters
     company supply section and S-4 logistics section to review all operational sustainment considerations
     and establish the sustainment plan. The team will refer to the following Collective Task to participate in
     sustainment plan development: Collective Task 71-CO-0433, Support Command Posts.

FINALIZE SHIFT CREWS

     1-279. The operations SGMs, working with the XO, principal staff officers, battle captains, and section
     NCOICs will finalize the shifts for each CP based on operational requirements and strengths of the staff.
     Once finalized, the operations SGM and section leads maintain the shifts and allow them to begin the
     process of training as a shift. See Individual Task 150-C2-5205, Lead a Shift Change.

FINALIZE SHIFT CREW RESPONSIBILITIES

     1-280. Once the crews are established, the operations SGMs, with the section NCOICs, will ensure that
     all CP responsibilities are set. For example, all sections know that they will give up one Soldier every
     shift to conduct security. See Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

PREPARE TO PERFORM IN A CONTESTED ELECTROMAGNETIC SPECTRUM

     1-281. CP crews must recognize and react to activity in the electromagnetic spectrum (EMS) that effects
     unit use of the EMS. Interference can be deliberately caused by enemy activity, or it can be from friendly
     or civilian uses of the EMS. CPs can also be targeted by the enemy through monitoring a CP’s
     electromagnetic emissions. CP crews and supporting signal Soldiers use Drills 11-CW-D5903 and 11-
     PLT-D5902, both titled React to Electromagnetic Interference, to prepare for this eventuality. CP leaders
     should use GTA 11-11-001, Emission Control Planning Aid, to assist them in preparing the CP for the
     contested EMS.

TABLE V: CONDUCT COMMAND POST REHEARSAL

     1-282. This table is conducted in concert with the staff Table V and in accordance with the unit CATS.
     The operations SGM will validate that each CP is operational, meets the commander’s intent and
     operational variables, and is ready for CP Table VI (Certification). The commander may opt to conduct

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

1-283. The commander directs the displacement of each CP in accordance with established unit SOP,
the commander’s intent, and operational variables. Prior to displacing, CPs will transfer control of any
current operations responsibilities to another CP and reassume them once established in the new location.
See Collective Tasks 71-BN-5201, Displace the Command Post, 71-BN-0050, Establish a Command
Post, and 71-BN-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control.

FINALIZE LOAD PLANS

1-284. As part of each section understanding their role within the CP, each section leader and section
NCOIC will ensure that all vehicles and equipment are able to emplace and displace as the mission,
commander’s intent, and orders from higher dictate. Refer to section SOPs and TMs for section
equipment. Refer to Collective Task 71-BN-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

FINALIZE THE COMMAND POST ARCHITECTURE

1-285. The CP architecture is a dynamic, flexible structure that consists of the elements of, and
interactions between, the OE, the CP, and the C2 system (people, processes, network, and CPs). The
operations SGM will finalize the structure of the CP based on the architecture in which it resides, based
on feedback from the commander, section leaders, and the results of Table V. This includes diagrams of
all equipment and personnel. 150-C2-5202, Develop a Command Post Layout.

IDENTIFY ELECTROMAGNETIC SIGNATURE

1-286. Working with the S-6, and S-3, each CP operations SGM identifies the CPs’ signature. This
includes visual, thermal, and electromagnetic signatures. For example, the operations SGM identifies
the heavy use of push to talk and cellular telephone usage within the CP and works with the XO and
commander to mitigate the risk those devices represent based on threat capabilities. Once
electromagnetic risks are identified, the operations SGM works to mitigate risks associated with the unit
digital footprint. Refer to Individual Task 113-25E-3003, Define the Electromagnetic Operations
Environment.

SECTION V – BATTALION DIGITAL CREW TRAINING TABLES

     1-322. Section V discusses the command and control training tables for the battalion digital crew.

COMMAND AND CONTROL TRAINING TABLES FOR THE
BATTALION DIGITAL CREW

     1-323. This chapter is designed to enable the commander and staff to train with the digital crew and
     evaluate the ability of staff and C2IS operators to integrate systems and generate the COP. The COP is
     a single display of relevant information tailored to unit requirements that simultaneously creates shared
     understanding horizontally and vertically throughout an organization. This shared understanding is
     measured by the ability of commanders and their staffs to make timely and effective decisions.

     1-324. Key trainers for the digital crew are the C2 information integrators (Soldiers expert in integrating
     C2 systems to establish the COP that lead digital crews), battle captains, KMOs, signal DMGs, and staff
     section leaders. It is also recommended that a trained C2 information integrator be assigned to every CP
     or node.

     1-325. The digital crew consists of the unit C2 information integrators, the COP manager, and the
     individual C2IS operators in all shifts of all CPs. The tables for the digital crew assume the individual
     C2IS operators are certified to operate their C2IS according to the appropriate proponent technical and
     training manuals. The C2 information integrator prepares the digital crew training and the certification

68         TC 6-0.2  30 July 2025


---

              Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Battalions

     program with the following Individual Tasks: 150-DMG-2030, Coordinate Command and Control
     Information Systems with Enablers, 150-DMG-2031 Develop a Digital Training Program (Unit,
     Individual, or Crew), and 150-DMG-2032, Execute the Digital Crew Training Program. The C2
     information integrator is focused on the integration of C2 information systems and is not trained to be
     the operator or expert of each C2IS. Execution of training tables occurs concurrently with other planned
     training to support organizational training requirements.

     1-326. The digital crew integrates C2 information systems to display information that enables the
     commander in the decision-making process. Led by the information integrator, the digital crew is
     responsible for advising the staff on the most effective techniques to integrate their C2IS. The digital
     crews coordinate with the COP manager to integrate critical information from each WFF into the COP
     to support the commander’s visualization.

     1-327. The composition of the digital crew at the battalion echelon is adjusted to support commander’s
     guidance. Considerations for guidance include training, education, experience of available personnel,
     and their ability to integrate their C2IS. The C2 information integrator and COP manager recommend
     composition and disposition of C2IS to the S-3 tailored to the OE. Planning factors include shift
     changeover, relocation of the CP, requirements for split operations, and mobile CP operations.

     1-328. The S-3 is responsible for the training and management of the digital crew to produce the unit’s
     COP through the execution of the digital crew training tables. The digital crew executes the training
     tables to support the commander’s guidance and training objectives under the supervision of the
     operations officer with support from the C2 information integrator and the S-6. Considerations for
     training include stability and proficiency of digital crews, available equipment calendar, and operational
     need. The digital training tables are designed to be executed sequentially as each table builds on skills
     learned in previous tables. Tables I focus through VI on the digital crew building proficiency with some
     requirements from the staff (e.g. graphics developed during MDMP). The S-3 should verify at the end
     of Table V training that the digital crew is prepared for the commander’s validation in Table VI. Tables
     VII through X focus on the employment and integration of the staff supported by the digital crews.

     1-329. Digital crews are rated as fully qualified, qualified, or unqualified with a maximum and minimum
     performance standard within each rating. The intent is for the digital crew to participate in the tables
     simultaneously as an organization.

          • Fully qualified – All assigned crew members have successfully completed Tables I-VI; at
                minimum, every member of the digital crew has completed these tables.

          • Qualified – 80% of the digital crew have successfully collectively completed Tables III-VI; at
                2846 minimum, every member of the digital crew has completed Tables I and II.

          • Unqualified – 1 or more crew members have not completed Tables I-II or more than 20% of
                the digital crewmembers in the organization’s headquarters have not completed Tables III-VI.

     1-330. Once a crew is fully qualified, commanders are advised to maintain digital crew integrity, similar
     to battle-rostering weapons system crews. When replacing crew members, the new crew member must
     be individually qualified on Tables I and II for the crew to maintain a qualified rating. If more than 20%
     of the crew members are replaced from the original fully qualified crew, the digital crew is considered
     unqualified until the new crew conducts Tables III through VI as a collective unit.

     establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

SET UP THE COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEM FOR OPERATIONS

    1-332. Given the variety of WFF information systems in the Army and version updates associated with
    each, the C2 information integrator will ensure that Soldiers of the digital crew are operating their
    MTO&E authorized systems in accordance with associated TMs and most recently approved software.
    The commander directs which systems and configuration to use based on mission requirements, the OE
    (both current and forecasted), unit capabilities, equipment/systems and versions through the Army
    fielding initiatives, and higher headquarters requirements. The C2 information integrator determines
    what tasks are selected from the TM to validate the operators’ abilities to employ the C2IS. Of note, the
    C2 information integrator should consider tasks based on the operator’s duties and responsibilities and
    the unit SOP. Individual Task 150-C2-5250, Employ a Command and Control Information System, and
    system TMs suggest tasks the operators should complete for this verification which should include:

         • Set up the C2IS for operation (Prepare the C2IS for Operations).
         • Employ the C2IS for operations.
         • Organize C2IS data.
         • Operate C2IS degraded (150-DMG-2020, Exercise Mission Command Under Degraded

              Conditions).

    1-333. The desired outcome for Table I is for the digital crew to understand the commander’s guidance
    for employment of C2IS and demonstrate proficiency on the respective systems. Additionally, the digital
    crew must be able to sustain operations throughout degraded conditions and understand use of analog
    tracking systems.

TABLE II: INTEGRATE COMMAND POST SYSTEMS

    1-334. The purpose of this table is to ensure that all digital crew members demonstrate that they can
    effectively transfer information and data to other systems on the entire network. Execution validates the
    C2IS integration across the staff as outlined in the KM SOPs to include setting permissions and sharing
    data. This table typically requires collaboration of the ‘digital triad’ (the C2 Information Integrator,
    Signal Digital Master Gunner [signal officer for units without the signal DMG], and KMO) in addition
    to appointed COP manager, S-3, and S-6. The appointed COP manager assists in the management of
    information for use in the COP. Digital crew members must be proficient at sharing information through
    the entire network at the conclusion of this table. If the entire network is not available or utilized for the
    table, the S-3, S-6, and C2 information integrator determine how to mitigate the associated risk and
    communicate their approach to the commander. Figure 1-32 below establishes the inputs, tasks to be
    accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

    Inputs                            Table II: Integrate Command Post Systems                Outputs

INTEGRATE C2 INFORMATION SYSTEMS WITH THE MISSION COMMAND WORKSTATION
(MCWS)

1-335. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2033, Integrate C2
Information Systems (C2IS) with the Mission Command Workstation (MCWS). Execution of this task
allows the C2IS operator associated with each WFF to share information through the mission command
workstation to update the COP. The C2 information integrator will ensure that each operator can publish
graphics in accordance with unit SOPs for dissemination to all authorized users. The C2 information
integrator also evaluates the C2IS operator’s ability to import and export graphics. The operations SGM,
battle captain, or battle NCO along with the C2 information integrator will confirm the digital crew’s
ability to import and share messages, reports, and overlays.

PERFORM TROUBLESHOOTING PROCEDURES ON C2IS

1-336. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-3003, Perform
Troubleshooting Procedures on the Command and Control Information System (C2IS). Operators use
equipment and software TMs and users guides to troubleshoot problems with C2IS hardware or software.
System operators will identify and correct system failure with successful functions check or escalate to
higher level support in accordance with the applicable TMs. When this task is complete, the workstation
is fully mission capable, or deficiencies and shortcomings are annotated and required maintenance
actions to be performed by someone other than the workstation's operator are identified. The C2
information integrator and COP manager will observe and evaluate the performance measures of this
task with the battle captain who will ensure that updates are provided in accordance with the digital SOP.

TABLE III: DEVELOP THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

1-337. Table III focuses on the development of the common operating picture through collaboration of
the digital crew, the COP manager, and staff operating within the commander’s guidance. Each member
of the digital crew will publish their respective digital efforts (graphics) through the network to populate
the COP, providing real-time situational awareness. The commander tailors the COP through guidance
to the staff WFF representatives who provide feedback to ensure achievement of commander’s intent.
The digital crew trains to support the commander’s and staff’s situational awareness through digitizing
relevant information using the C2IS to display the digital COP. The updated COP reflects the most
current events tailored to CCIR to support the commander’s decision-making process. Figure 1-33 below
establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

ESTABLISH THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

    1-338. The C2 information integrator and COP manager will use Individual Task 150-C2-5315,
    Establish the Common Operational Picture. This task is critical to the success of the staff and digital
    crew and may require coordination with the COP manager, battle captain, or S-3 to validate the digital
    crew’s ability to produce the COP. The COP manager and battle captain update the COP based on the
    mission, relevant information imported from C2IS, and information from other sources to enable the
    commander’s decision making.

PREPARE THE DIGITAL COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

    1-339. The C2 information integrator leads the digital crew to develop a list of items to display on the
    COP, and practice putting them in appropriate C2IS, including:

         • Boundaries.
         • Phase lines.
         • Objectives.
         • Maneuver graphics.
         • Fire support coordination measures (FSCM).
         • Airspace coordinating measures (ACM).
         • Known and suspected enemy locations.
         • Supply routes and depots.
         • Decision points.

    1-340. The digital crew will determine which elements of the COP will be automatically populated on
    the COP versus those the crew will have to enter manually and practice doing so.

    1-341. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2035, Prepare the Digital
    Common Operational Picture, to guide the crew through Table III.

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

    1-342. The KMO will inform the operations SGM, C2 information integrator, and section and cell
    leaders of mission-related information and KM requirements. The COP, as part of the unit’s combined
    KM plan, enables the provision of relevant information to the right person at the right time and in a
    usable format to facilitate understanding and decision-making. The C2 information integrator supervises
    the digital crew in establishing this key component of the KMP. The C2 information integrator
    participates in developing a KMP and digital portions of the unit SOP. The C2 information integrator
    will oversee staff sections configuring their systems and equipment to conform to the information

EXECUTE BATTLE DRILLS

1-345. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2015, Execute Battle Drills
Using Command and Control Information Systems (C2IS). The successful unit will have an established
list of approved battle drills that are published in the unit SOP (CP, plans, and digital sections). The staff
reviews and executes battle drills in accordance with SOP and commander’s guidance for training. This
process is supervised by the COP manager, operations SGM, or S-3. The battle captain determines if
execution of the battle drills achieves commander’s information requirements outlined in the SOP. The
staff verifies the battle drills supporting their WFF through screening criteria, setting conditions for
successful operations.

SHARE A PLAN USING COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEMS

1-346. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-3006, Share a Plan Using the
Command and Control Information Systems. The staff assists the commander’s visualization through
use of C2IS visualization aides including maps, charts, and tables. The C2 information integrator assists
the COP manager, battle captain, or S-3 to select a briefing format that supports the commander’s
guidance and mission requirements.

PRESENT A COMMAND POST UPDATE BRIEFING

     1-347. The C2 information integrator and COP manager will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2016,
     Present Command Post Update Briefing. The CP update brief is the culmination of the integration and
     information sharing to create situational and shared understanding. The battle captain, S-3, or
     commander validates the update briefing to ensure it meets the commander’s guidance. A briefing
     manager is designated by the XO or S-3 to organize and control the briefing (typically the battle captain
     or battle NCO) in each CP. The designated briefing manager guides staff preparation for the update in
     coordination with the COP manager and C2 information integrator. Briefing preparation techniques
     traditionally focus on gaining efficiency by leveraging C2IS capabilities throughout the presentation.
     Briefing concludes with a rehearsal by warfighting function.

TABLE V: CONDUCT COMMAND AND CONTROL NETWORK
REHEARSAL

     1-348. The network rehearsal presents the last opportunity in digital training tables for digital crews to
     refine their processes before commanders certify their digital crews in Table VI. Table V is scenario
     driven and evaluates the digital crew’s ability to use digital systems to support the operations process.
     Retraining may be required through multiple scenarios to ensure compliance with battle drills and refine
     unit SOPs. At the conclusion of Table V the digital crew should be operating at full capacity.

     1-349. Once the digital crew successfully completes all requirements of Table V, the C2 information
     integrator reports to the S-3, the operations SGM, and each battle captain that the digital crew is prepared
     for the certification event (Table VI). Figure 1-35 below establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished
     during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

     products, maintaining the COP, creating storyboards, sharing information, and making recommendations
     to the commander. Digital crews are the experts in the integration of information and are fully prepared

1-354. The COP manager will use Collective Task 71-BN-5319, Establish the Common Operational

1-355. Upon successful completion of Table VI, the commander certifies the digital crews. Any crew
considered qualified or above is proficient at system integration, COP development, and supporting the
staff’s synchronization of operations. Crew members are able to provide integration advice to the staff
during Tables VII through X and fully integrate their systems into the unit’s operations process.

1-356. Digital crew members will use their C2IS to battle track, support the staff, execute battle drills,
maintain running estimates, conduct briefings, and manage the commander's information requirements

Chapter 1

     1-357. The outcome of Tables I through VI is that digital crews operate as cohesive units. Each system
     operator understands and competently performs in their WFF staff role to support the mission.
     Completing Table VI indicates that the digital crew is prepared to support the commander in the conduct
     of LSCO. Upon completion of Table VI and the certification of the C2IS, the digital crew, led by the C2
     information integrator and COP manager, transitions to Tables VII through X.

     1-358. Tables VII through X are designed to train the staff, CPs, and digital crew as an integrated team.
     The certified digital crew will assist and enhance the staff’s ability to conduct the operations process. As
     the unit transitions into Tables VII through X, the C2 information integrator also transitions from
     controlling the certification process to serving as an advisor to the command team. The C2 information
     integrator now serves as the subject matter expert on digital training and integration.

     1-359. Digital crews must be familiar with all aspects of their digital systems and understand how they
     add value to the commander and staff through their C2IS. While the digital crew is considered an integral
     part of the staff for Tables VII through X, the digital crew’s proficiency and actions are evaluated
     separately from the staff sections. This will allow the commander to validate the digital crew’s
     qualification ratings separate from staff for meeting training objectives or retraining purposes, and to
     identify and isolate problems should they arise.

     1-360. The commander uses the tables VII-IX as a means of integrating the digital crew and the systems
     into the headquarters and staff functions to obtain a higher level of proficiency. The C2 information
     integrator and digital triad works with the staff to refine the digital SOP and develop a retraining plan to
     ensure that the digital crew members maintain proficiency and operate effectively and efficiently within
     their staff section.

     1-361. Upon completion of certifications in Table VI, the unit will receive an order from the headquarters
     responsible for the battalion EXEVAL in Table X. The digital crew will use the information from this
     order to train tables VII through IX.

TABLE VII: INTEGRATE THE COMMAND AND CONTROL
SYSTEM

     1-362. Table VII is led by the C2 information integrator operating under commander, XO, and S-3

     into the digital crew as needed. It begins with the establishment of the CP and digital systems to include
     staff coordination with horizontal and vertical elements. This table includes establishing the network,
     C2 system integration, and establishing and disseminating the COP. Figure 1-37 below establishes the
     inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

ESTABLISH A COMMAND POST

1-363. The XO will direct the S-3 and operations SGM to prepare to establish the unit’s CPs per unit
SOP. The COP manager and C2 information integrator focus on establishing digital capabilities of the
CPs while the remaining staff focus on establishing CP. The digital crew’s responsibility is to establish
the network within the CP. The unit will refer to Collective Task 71-BN-0050, Establish a Command
Post.

PROCESS RELEVANT INFORMATION

1-364. The XO and S-3 provide guidance on how the staff organizes to support the commander’s
exercise of C2. The staff provides relevant information to the commander to answer information
requirements. The digital crew will support the staff in processing relevant information in accordance
with the standard established in Collective Task 71-BN-5315, Process Relevant Information.

DISPLAY THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

1-365. This task, as with all the tasks in Tables VII-X, is performed in conjunction with executing a
tactical task the unit must perform in support of the higher headquarters order. The commander
establishes requirements for information displayed on the common operational picture. The digital crew
establishes C2IS to manage and share information based on SOPs and mission requirements. The staff
maintains running estimates focused on the mission and commander’s guidance under the direction of
the XO. The digital crew, under the guidance of the COP manager, produce the COP using C2IS systems
tailored to operational requirements and guidance. The command will use Collective Task 71-BN-5319,
Establish the Common Operational Picture, for the execution of this task.

DISSEMINATE THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE AND EXECUTION INFORMATION

1-366. The staff, led by the XO, directs the execution of the KMP and distribution of the COP to
subordinate CPs to create and maintain shared understanding. The battle captain and COP manager
update the COP to ensure the most relevant information is displayed while the S-3 directs the COP
manager and C2 information integrator to post and disseminate the COP across the C2 network. The
COP manager and C2 information integrator ensure the COP is accurately disseminated to lower
echelons across the network. The command will reference Collective Task 71-BN-5319, Establish the
Common Operational Picture.

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

1-367. The C2 information integrator supervises the digital crew in establishing the COP – a key
component of the KMP. The C2 information integrator participates in refining the KMP and digital
portions of the unit SOP. The C2 information integrator will oversee the staff sections and C2IS operators

DEVELOP RUNNING ESTIMATES

    1-369. The digital crew supports the staff in conducting Collective Task 71-BN-5144, Develop a
    Running Estimate. The staff led by the XO, continues to update running estimates in their C2IS
    throughout operations to integrate relevant information into the COP.

CONDUCT THE RAPID DECISION-MAKING SYNCHRONIZATION PROCESS

    1-370. The digital crew employs C2IS to conduct automated coordination with higher, adjacent, and
    subordinate units and any unified action partners to implement changes to the plan in support of the staff
    and commander making changes; and then implementing the changes in digital products. For example,
    the sustainment cell digital system operators will use their systems to coordinate changes to logistics
    priorities with supporting sustainment units. The command references Collective Task 71-BN-5002,
    Conduct Rapid Decision-Making and Synchronization Process.

CONDUCT AN OPERATIONS SYNCHRONIZATION MEETING

    1-371. The command conducts an operations synchronization meeting electronically, including all CPs
    and relevant subordinate units using Collective Task 71-BN-5135, Conduct an Operations
    Synchronization Meeting, as a guide. Synchronization of staff efforts and priorities ensures productivity
    and unity of effort. The S-3, or designated representative, guides the staff through managing products to
    sustain the unity of effort. The staff provides updates to unit leaders through battle rhythm events such
    as daily briefings and staff updates to synchronize current and future events.

TABLE IX: COMMAND AND CONTROL WFF VALIDATION

     1-372. This table synchronizes the staff, digital crew, CP, and the commander to conduct the operations

     1-373. The digital crew establishes the C2 network in support of the staff and CP crew setting up the CP,

VALIDATE THE DIGITAL STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURE

     1-374. Under the authority of the commander, the C2 information integrator will validate the digital
     systems portion of the unit SOP using Collective Task 71-BN-5330, Conduct Knowledge Management,
     ensuring that the critical SOP requirements below are met.

          • Produce and disseminate mission orders through the C2IS.

          • Display statuses.
          • Track and report operations and sustainment.

ASSIST THE COMMANDER IN EXECUTING TACTICAL OPERATIONS

     1-375. The headquarters conducts the operations process by integrating, analyzing, and synthesizing data
     to determine relevant information for action. This commander and staff process occurs internally and
     externally to the headquarters and simultaneously across the C2IS to achieve shared understanding at
     echelon and with adjacent units. The digital crew maintains a consistent and accurate COP to enable this
     process. The digital crew members will complete Individual Task 150-C2-5131, Assist the Commander
     in Executing Operations, and Collective Task 71-BN-5103, Control Operations.
