                    Figure 2-16. Brigade Staff Table VI: Conduct Staff Certification

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

1-590. The staff must achieve a “go” in all performance measures under Collective Task 71-BDE-5100,
Conduct Operations Process for Command and Control. The staff will conduct the operations process
fully supporting the commander and the mission. Each section will maintain running estimates and
recommend any plan variations and changes in a timely manner to the commander. The staff will conduct
the operations process while conducting tasks in their standard METL, A-METL, or other tasks assigned
by the higher headquarters or the commander.

EXECUTE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

1-591. The XO will ensure that the established battle rhythm is executed in support of the mission and
the commander’s intent. The XO will ensure that meetings provide outcomes that directly support
decisions that the commander must make to achieve the desired end state. See Collective Task 71-BDE-
5125, Implement the Battle Rhythm, and Individual Task 150-LDR-5011, Establish Boards and Working
Groups.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

1-592. Staff personnel execute several routine and recurring tasks to assist the CP personnel to establish,
operate, and displace the CP. The staff, under the guidance of the XO, develop processes and procedures
for the execution of these tasks and capture them in the CP SOP. See ATP 6-0.5 and Collective Task 71-
BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

CREATE SHARED UNDERSTANDING

1-593. The XO and principal staff officers will use running estimates, C2IS, and meetings to ensure that
shared understanding is achieved across the staff and with subordinate units. This shared understanding
encapsulates the commander’s vision, guidance, intent, desired end state and the OE. This shared
understanding is displayed in the COP by the unit C2 system. Staff members use Individual Task 150-

30 July 2025                                               TC 6-0.2                                                           127


---

Chapter 2

     LDR-5007, Establish a Shared Understanding, and Collective Task 71-BDE-5007, Establish a Shared
     Understanding, as references for this task.

CONTROL OPERATIONS

     1-594. The staff controls operations by arranging forces in time, space, and purpose to produce
     maximum relative combat power at the decisive point. They use such tools as staff estimates, the COP,
     DSM, DST, operations schedule, event matrix, and graphic control measures to constantly evaluate
     progress of the operation against what was planned to occur. They take actions within their authority and
     area of expertise to bring combat power to bear and seek guidance from or make recommendations to
     the commander or higher headquarters when a situation exceeds the staff’s authority. The staff should
     use Collective Task 71-BDE-5103, Control Operations.

TRANSFER COMMAND AND CONTROL BETWEEN COMMAND POSTS

     1-595. The staff will pass control of the operation from one CP to another during the execution of Table
     VI, either to displace a CP or in reaction to an attack on a CP. While doing so, the commander and XO
     will ensure that the C2 system maintains continuity of C2. The staff in each CP will successfully transfer
     control of the functions for which they are primarily responsible from their CP to another CP per unit
     SOP, and then resume control when capability is restored. The XO works with the operations SGM on
     which systems get shut down and then reestablished, and in what order, to ensure C2 is maintained
     throughout the process. The XO will use Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5250, Maintain Continuity of
     Command and Control, and 71-BDE-5202, Conduct Transfer Control of Operations.

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

     1-596. The unit will displace each CP at least once during this training event. Critical aspects of C2, such
     as contact with higher headquarters and subordinate units, must be maintained during displacement.
     Displacements are planned to ensure the main CP is stationary during critical phases of the operation.
     All other CPs will displace per unit SOP at least once during the certification event (e.g. TAC and field
     trains). Refer to Collective Task 71-BN-5201, Displace the Command Post, and 71-BDE-5105, Conduct
     Quartering Party Operations.

SENSITIVE COMPARTMENTALIZED INFORMATION FACILITY OPERATIONS
[OPERATIONAL]

     1-597. Not all brigades have the personnel or equipment to establish a SCIF, but for those that do, the
     SSO or S-2 establishes controls the SCIF and T-SCIF and manages, ensures appropriate accreditation
     for, and reviews and manages emergency plans for SCIFs and T-SCIFs. The SSO coordinates access to
     the SCIF or T-SCIF and maintains records of personnel clearances and classified document registers and
     manages special access program (SAP) access. Refer to Collective Task 34-TM-3090, Manage Special
     Security Office (SSO) Functions.

FOLLOWING CERTIFICATION

     1-598. Upon completion of Table VI and staff certification, the staff, led by the XO and principal staff
     officers, and under the guidance of the commander, transitions to Tables VII through X. These tables are
     a more graduate level of the C2 WFF. In these, the staff integrates external elements such as liaisons and
     multinational partners, the CPs train together as one unit C2 systems to conduct the operations process
     and support the commander’s decision making, and conduct more interactions with higher, lower,
     adjacent, supported, and supporting headquarters. To do this successfully, all staff members must
     understand the roles and functions of not just their section and CP, but of all sections and CPs in the
     brigade’s C2 system. Tables VII-IX lead the command to the CTE of the headquarters in Table X.

TABLE VII: INTEGRATE THE COMMAND AND CONTROL
SYSTEM

     1-599. Table VII begins with receipt of an order from higher headquarters for the unit’s external
     evaluation. Depending on the unit type and the nature of the operation planned for the EXEVAL, the

RECEIVE AND INTEGRATE LIAISONS AND ATTACHMENTS

    1-600. As the mission requires and in accordance with unit capabilities, the unit receives liaisons and
    attachments from unified action partners. The XO and principal staff officers will ensure that the
    additional personnel have the network and other requirements in place for connectivity and assisting the
    commander in the conduct of LSCO. The XO will determine the extent that each section/cell leader will
    participate in the reception and integration of these liaisons and attachments. Once the liaisons or
    attachments are set, the command will conduct staff tailoring and task organizing. The unit will complete
    Collective Task 71-BDE-5019, Integrate Liaisons and Attachments.

CONDUCT THE MILITARY DECISION-MAKING PROCESS

    1-601. Once the liaisons and attachments are received and integrated, the XO will lead the staff through
    the MDMP. The XO will use the order issued by higher headquarters for the external evaluation in Table
    X. The staff will use Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5111, Conduct the Military Decision-Making Process,
    and 71-BDE-5009, Develop a Decision Support Template, and Individual Task 150-C2-5139, Develop
    a Decision Support Matrix.

TASK ORGANIZE FOR OPERATIONS

    1-602. With the possible addition of units and personnel in preparation for either a training exercise or
    LSCO, the staff will recommend a task organization for the operation that coincides with the array of
    forces in the COA the commander decides to implement during MDMP. The command will use

Chapter 2

     Collective Task 71-BDE-5123, Task Organize for Operations. Brigade combat teams and functional or
     multifunctional brigades assigned responsibility for an area within the division or corps rear area should
     also perform Collective Task 71-BDE-5138, Manage Terrain, as part of this table.

COORDINATE EFFORTS WITH HIGHER, LOWER, ADJACENT AND SUPPORTING UNITS

     1-603. As part of conducting the scenario driven MDMP, the XO will ensure that the cells and sections
     are properly leveraging and tasking higher, lower, adjacent, and supporting units as appropriate. The XO
     will further ensure that the battle captains and cell leads are relaying all information as a command to
     higher, lower, and adjacent, supported, and supporting units based on mission requirements
     (understanding that the relaying of information may be notional for training purposes). The XO further
     directs all relevant information is shared throughout the staff. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5121,
     Establish Coordination and Liaison.

ASSESS THE TACTICAL SITUATION AND OPERATION

     1-604. The commander approved an assessment plan in the Commander Table VII. The staff integrates
     that assessment plan with training objectives, the commander’s DSM, DST, and desired end state. The
     XO ensures all CPs contribute to assessment. The XO informs the commander of any overlaps, conflicts,
     or issues. The XO and principal staff officers use Collective Task 71-BDE-5130, Assess the Tactical
     Situation and Operation.

MANAGE COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEMS

     1-605. The brigade manages C2ISs to support reliable, timely information flow in support of operations,
     within the authorities granted it by unit type and/or mission. The command plans, engineers, installs,
     operates, maintains, and protects DoD Information Network – Army (DODIN-A). The command
     supports operations by executing enterprise management, network assurance, and content management,
     as directed. The command supervises network operations, network transport and information services,
     spectrum management, visual information operations, and communications security (COMSEC) in order
     to ensure the transport, security, storage, display, management, integration, and maintenance of
     information, communications systems and networks. Refer to Collective Task 71-BDE-5118, Manage
     Command, Control, and Information Systems.

CONDUCT KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

     1-606. The commander ensures that the staff are correctly conducting KM and IM. The staff, led by the
     S-6 and the KMO revise the KMP. The KM working group assesses new information requirements for
     attachments and other partners (if any), and updates the KMP leading to the Validation Exercise in Table
     X. All CP/staff sections contribute to developing the KMP. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5330, Conduct
     Knowledge Management. The staff should also practice steps associated with Collective Task 71-BDE-
     5010, Protect Information, and Individual Task 150-C2-2222, Request Foreign Disclosure.

TABLE VIII: SYNCHRONIZE OPERATIONS

     1-607. The XO synchronizes WFFs. The table is event and scenario driven in a manner that the unit can
     work towards MET proficiency with collective tasks T&EOs selected by the commander. The venue and
     facility location are determined by the commander in accordance with the unit CATS and mission
     requirements. If the training scenario for Table X EXEVAL calls for the brigade to work closely with
     unified action partners, the brigade staff will incorporate partner operations and information
     requirements into the brigade’s plans, orders, and procedures during this table. The staff should practice
     the full gamut of battle rhythm reports and events during the execution of Table VIII. Figure 2-18 on
     page 130 establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for
     the table.

EXERCISE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

1-608. With the inclusion of liaisons and attachments (including unified action partners if the training
scenario includes them), the XO will integrate their requirements into the battle rhythm and battle drills.
The battle rhythm is based on unit capabilities, mission requirements, and the commander’s intent. Now
that the staff is working with other live units instead of simulating their interactions, the XO may have
to adjust the battle rhythm to meet the needs of higher, lower, supported, and supporting units, and
unified action partners. Commands build upon the battle rhythm built in tables IV through VI. Refer to
Collective Task 71-BDE-5125, Implement the Battle Rhythm.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

1-609. With all attachments, liaisons, and supporting units participating in the exercise, the commander
leads the staff through the operations process. The XO will use Collective Task 71-BDE-5100, Conduct
the Operations Process for Command and Control, to assess their proficiency in conducting the
operations process in connection with the collective tasks being performed for the training event. The
staff should also execute the battle drills developed prior to Table VI when conducting the Execute phase
of the operations process.

CONTROL OPERATIONS

1-610. The staff assists the commander in controlling operations by arranging forces in time, space, and
purpose to produce maximum relative combat power at the decisive point. They take actions within their
authority and area of expertise to bring combat power to bear and seek guidance from or make
recommendations to the commander or higher headquarters when a situation exceeds the staff’s
authority. They use such tools as staff estimates, the COP, DSM, DST, operations schedule, event matrix,
and graphic control measures to constantly evaluate progress of the operation against what was planned
to occur. The staff should use Collective Task 71-BDE-5103, Control Operations.

CONDUCT AN OPERATIONS SYNCHRONIZATION MEETING

1-611. The current operations staff should conduct multiple repetitions of the operations synchronization
meeting and conducting an AAR after each one with the primary goal of making the meetings both
effective and efficient. Identify information that was presented that was unnecessary and coach
participants to brief their information as succinctly as they can while covering what is relevant. See
Collective Task 71-BDE-5135, Conduct an Operations Synchronization Meeting.

PROCESS RELEVANT INFORMATION

1-612. Relevant information is analyzed information that commanders need to make decisions or that
staff officers need to perform their duties. Relevant information includes not just the information
requirements identified during planning, but also exceptional information that wasn’t understood to be

30 July 2025                                                 TC 6-0.2                                   131


---

Chapter 2

     relevant during planning but becomes relevant during execution of operations. The staff will display and
     share relevant information based on security and mission requirements and disseminates it to other units
     or partners to whom it will also be relevant. The display is tied to the COP and nested with the
     commander’s DSM. If unified action partners are present, the command will ensure security and foreign
     disclosure policies and procedures are met. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5315, Process Relevant
     Information.

CONDUCT REHEARSALS

     1-613. Conducting the operations process includes the XO leading the staff to perform a combined arms
     rehearsal. The rehearsal nests all warfighting functions and the capabilities of the unit, attachments,
     available support, or unified action partners for the mission. Staff leaders will conduct functional
     rehearsals (for example, the sustainment rehearsal) in accordance with commander’s guidance and the
     brigade SOP. The commander’s DSM and the plan are updated based on the outcomes of the rehearsals.
     See Collective Task 71-BDE-5122, Perform a Rehearsal.

TABLE IX: COMMAND AND CONTROL WFFVALIDATION
EXERCISE REHEARSAL

     1-614. The validation exercise rehearsal is conducted in accordance with the unit CATS and the
     commander’s intent. This event is conducted in a real or simulated OE which the unit is allocated against
     for a given contingency. The exercise is conducted using 24-hour operations and lasts more than two
     days. The exercise will also be based on the order issued prior to Table VII by the evaluating headquarters
     for Table X. The intent is that this exercise match as closely as possible the training circumstances and
     requirements anticipated in Table X, the C2WFF validation exercise. At the conclusion of executing
     Table IX, functional and integrating cell chiefs conduct informal internal AARs prior to the
     commander’s unit AAR. Figure 2-19 on next page establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during
     this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

     1-615. The staff conducts the operations process while performing METs. See Collective Task 71-BDE-
     5100, Conduct the Operations Process for Command and Control; 71-BDE-5002, Conduct Rapid
     Decision-Making and Synchronization Process, and 71-BDE-5103, Control Operations.

CONDUCT KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

     1-616. The XO ensures the staff is conducting KM and IM to standard. Both are tied to running estimates
     and the display of the COP. The management of these reflects the commander’s intent, the DSM, the
     mission, and orders from higher headquarters. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5330, Conduct Knowledge
     Management.

REVISE THE PLAN

     1-617. During the course of the exercise, the brigade will have to react to opportunities and unforeseen
     challenges. The staff re-synchronizes the operation and revises the plan, ensuring the revised plan
     complies with commander's guidance and intent and accurately reflects updated circumstances. Refer to
     Collective Task 71-BDE-5124, Revise the Plan.

EMPLACE AND DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

     1-618. The unit displaces all CPs at least once each during this event. The command maintains continuity
     of C2 during the CP displacement. The staff, led by the XO and under the direction of the commander
     completes Collective Tasks:

          • 71-BDE-5201, Displace the Command Post.
          • 71-BDE-0050, Establish a Command Post.
          • 71-BDE-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-619. CP personnel execute several routine and recurring tasks to establish, operate, and move the CP.
     With the assistance of the staff, commanders develop processes and procedures for the execution of these
     tasks and encapsulate them into the SOPs to govern CP operations. See ATP 6-0.5 and Collective Task
     71-BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

CONDUCT SENSITIVE COMPARTMENTALIZED INFORMATION FACILITY OPERATIONS
[OPTIONAL]

     1-620. Not all brigades have the personnel or equipment to establish SCIF, but for those that do, the SSO
     or S-2 establishes controls for the SCIF and T-SCIF and manages, ensures appropriate accreditation for,
     and reviews and manages emergency plans for SCIFs and T-SCIFs. The SSO coordinates access to the
     SCIF or T-SCIF and maintains records of personnel clearances and classified document registers and
     manages SAP access. Refer to Collective Task 34-TM-3090, Manage Special Security Office (SSO)
     Functions.

CONDUCT AFTER ACTION REVIEWS

     1-621. The commander conducts a formal AAR at the conclusion of Table IX and provides guidance to
     the staff on final adjustments to make prior to executing the Table X Validation Exercise. Refer to
     Individual Task 150-C2-5133, Conduct a Formal After Action Review.

CONDUCT THE OPERATIONS PROCESS

     1-623. The staff conducts the operations process while the unit performs tasks on their unit Standardized
     METL, or other tasks as directed by the commander. The XO and staff will complete Collective Tasks
     71-BDE-5100, Conduct the Operations Process for Command and Control, and 71-BDE-5002, Conduct
     Rapid Decision-Making and Synchronization Process, and 71-BDE-5103, Control Operations.

CONDUCT KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT

     1-624. The XO and principal staff officers will use the COP, running estimates, C2IS, and meetings to
     maintain shared understanding across the force. The KMO will lead the KM working group to update
     and implement the brigade KMP as changes to the situation drive changes in information requirements.
     Shared understanding is based on the commander’s vision, guidance, intent, desired end state, and the
     OE. Refer to Collective Task 71-BDE-5330, Conduct Knowledge Management.

REVISE THE PLAN

     1-625. The staff will use the battle rhythm and the DST & DSM to make and implement execution
     decisions during the course of Table X. If variances are detected, then the staff will use the RDSP to
     enable adjustment decisions and re-synchronize the operation. The XO will ensure that the boards,
     working groups, and meetings have outcomes that directly support decisions that the command must
     make to achieve the desired end state. The meetings outlined above are nested with the unit battle rhythm
     and support the higher command’s decision-making. Refer to Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5124, Revise
     the Plan, and 71-BDE-5002, Conduct Rapid Decision-Making and Synchronization Process.

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

     1-626. The unit is required to displace each CP at least once during this training event. While doing so,
     the commander and XO will ensure that they maintain continuity of C2. The staff will successfully
     transfer C2 from the main CP to the alternate CP, and then back to the main CP upon completion of the
     displacement. Similarly, the other CPs will hand off responsibilities, move, reestablish the CP, and
     resume responsibilities. The battle captain and operations SGM direct the staff on when to relinquish
     responsibilities to counterparts in another CP, and to begin preparation for movement. The staff will use
     Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5201, Displace the Command Post, 71-BDE-5202, Conduct Transfer Control
     of Operations, and 71-BDE-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-627. The staff execute routine and recurring tasks to establish, operate, and move the CPs in
     accordance with the unit SOP to ensure support to the commander’s decision-making. The staff executes
     SOP-based battle drills as exercise events trigger them (for example, the main CP receives incoming
     artillery fire). See ATP 6-0.5 and Collective Task 71-BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

EXECUTE THE BATTLE RHYTHM

     1-628. The XO ensures the staff execute established battle rhythm and adjusts the battle rhythm as
     exercise events interfere with its execution. Refer Collective Task 71-BDE-5125, Implement the Battle
     Rhythm.

CONDUCT SENSITIVE COMPARTMENTALIZED INFORMATION FACILITY OPERATIONS
[OPTIONAL]

     1-629. Not all brigades have the personnel or equipment to establish a SCIF, but for those that do, SSO)
     / S-2 establishes controls for the SCIF and the T-SCIF and manages, ensures appropriate accreditation
     for, and reviews and manages emergency plans for the SCIFs and T-SCIFs. The SSO coordinates access
     to the SCIF or T-SCIF and maintains records of personnel clearances and classified document registers
     and manages SAP access. Refer to Collective Task 34-TM-3090, Manage Special Security Office
     Functions.

     1-630. The outcome of Tables VII through X is external validation that the staff in each CP, with external
     augmenters and liaisons, is prepared to support the brigade commander’s decision-making and execution

Chapter 2

     of C2 during the conduct of LSCO. Each staff section/cell and CP understands and operates according
     to their assigned roles and responsibilities. The staff performs the operations process seamlessly.
     Training shortfalls were identified by Table IX and corrected before Table X.

     1-631. Completing Table X indicates that the command is prepared to conduct METL tasks at or above
     the T level and is prepared at an advanced level to conduct operations. The outcome of Table X is that
     the evaluating commander has validated that the brigade staff is prepared to support the brigade
     commander in the execution of commanding forces and controlling operations during operations.

SECTION IV – BRIGADE COMMAND POST TRAINING TABLES

     1-632. Section IV discusses the command and control training tables for the brigade command post.

COMMAND AND CONTROL TRAINING TABLES FOR THE
BRIGADE COMMAND POSTS

     1-633. The brigade employs the main CP, the TAC CP, and may establish other CPs based on mission
     and capabilities. Commanders will ensure that all CPs are trained in accordance with unit TO&E design,
     manning, and design capabilities. This training includes that the personnel in each command post
     understand the roles and responsibilities of all CPs, how they interact with each other, and how each CP
     interacts with higher, lower, adjacent, supported, and supporting headquarters.

     1-634. For the purpose of this TC, the CP training audience consists of the junior officers and NCOs on
     the staff in each CP that conduct the routine functions that keep the CPs functioning during 24-hour
     operations. This section walks the commander, XO, battle captains and operations SGM through the
     tables needed to train, certify, and validate CPs. This section does not cover every aspect of training, nor
     will it pinpoint exactly how one trains each collective or individual task. Commanders and leaders
     wanting more information on training the force will reference the T&EOs for each task and FM 7-0 for
     assistance in conducting training management.

     1-635. The XO and principal staff officers ensure that each section NCOIC devotes a portion of their
     section training to CP operations under the guidance of the battle captains and operations SGMs.
     Commanders empower the NCOs in the brigade to complete the tasks in these tables.

     1-636. The operations SGM is the senior NCO in each CP responsible for monitoring and supervising
     the performance of the enlisted operations staff. The operations SGMs assist the operations officer in all
     matters pertaining to the operations process to include planning, rehearsals, and monitoring and
     controlling 522 operations in the COIC. In addition, the operations SGM assists the CP officer in charge
     with CP administration to include the following:

          • Establishing the CP.
          • Displacing the CP.
          • Providing CP security.
          • Maintaining continuity of CP operations.
          • Executing sleep plans.
          • Managing stress within the CP.

     1-637. The operations SGM in each CP directs section NCOs to manage guard rosters, sleep plans, and
     shift schedules as well as to maintain discipline in and around the CP. The operations SGM works hand
     in hand with the headquarters company NCOs to fulfill the logistics requirements, tactical employment,
     and security of the CP.

     1-638. Brigade CP Tables I through VI are directed at the individuals, teams, and cells led by the
     operations SGM. The culmination of this training comes in Table V, rehearsal and Table VI,
     certification, as the headquarters comes together to work as a collective unit. There are no external
     attachments required for the execution of these tables. The XO is the authority, in coordination with the
     battle captain and operations SGM, to transition the staff from CP Tables I through V. Tables I-II are

136  TC 6-0.2  30 July 2025

              Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

designed to occur on a continuous basis as new personnel arrive to the unit. The XO and operations
SGMs will verify training proficiency of the CP in Table V, the rehearsal for certification.

1-639. The brigade commander certifies each CP at Table VI allowing them to transition to the more
advanced brigade CP Tables VII through X.

1-640. Tables VII through X build on basic CP competency and may (depending on unit type and
mission) require inclusion of augmentees, unified action partners, emplacement and displacement,
operational security, SCIF operations (as needed), and continuous 24-hour operations.

initial CP training through basic understanding of duties and responsibilities, under the supervision of

the XO, battle captains, and operations SGM. Figure 2-21 on page 136 establishes the inputs, tasks to be

accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

INVENTORY COMMAND POST EQUIPMENT

     1-642. Here all members of the CP familiarize themselves on all section equipment. Staff section leaders
     will train their Soldiers using individual and section collective tasks as defined by the appropriate
     proponent. For example, the S-6 NCOIC conducts an inventory of all the equipment their section is
     responsible for in accordance with the MTO&E. Leaders take note of what equipment is required to run
     each CP (node) and where there are gaps. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command
     Post Operations.

VERIFY COMMAND POST EQUIPMENT READINESS

     1-643. The section leaders ensure that all members of the section understand how their assigned
     equipment operates both internally to their section and within the CP as a whole. Section NCOICs verify
     the usability and readiness of their equipment in accordance with the equipment Preventative
     Maintenance Checks and Services (PMCS) and other equipment services found in each piece of
     equipment’s Technical Manual (TM). Each section NCOIC verifies all assigned equipment according to
     the TM and report any shortages per unit SOP. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct
     Command Post Operations.

Chapter 2

IDENTIFY SUSTAINMENT REQUIREMENTS FOR THE COMMAND POSTS

     1-644. The operations SGMs work with staff section NCOs in charge to develop CP sustainment
     requirements for current and future operations. Each operations SGM works with the headquarters
     company to fulfill those requirements in accordance with Collective Task 71-CO-0433, Support
     Command Posts.

SCHEDULE MAINTENANCE REQUIREMENTS

     1-645. The Section NCOICs will, based on operational requirements, manning constraints, and guidance
     from headquarters company commander, schedule PMCS activities on a routine basis. Refer to
     Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

TABLE II: IDENTIFY COMMAND POST CHARACTERISTICS

     1-646. The section NCOICs and operations SGMs continue to supervise the CP Soldiers as they refine
     the basic and intermediate skills of their sections. Section NCOICs have primary responsibility for this
     table. The staff equip and organize CPs to manage operations for extended periods of time. CP personnel
     use information systems and equipment to support 24-hour operations. As such, CP personnel and
     equipment must be protected and sustained. This requires an effective SOP and personnel trained on CP
     administration to include the following:

          • Establishing the CP.
          • Displacing the CP.
          • Providing security.
          • Maintaining continuity of operations.
          • Executing sleep plans.
          • Managing stress.

     auditable outputs for the table.

IDENTIFY COMMAND POST REQUIREMENTS

     1-648. Once the commander establishes the C2IS requirements (see Commander Training Table I), the
     operations SGMs work with the S-6, the KM team, the COIC, and the XO to conceptualize how the CP
     will look. This step of the tables is meant as a tabletop drill or sketch only. The operations SGM will
     lead the discussion as the other attendees provide inputs. The operations SGM works with the CP systems
     and other resources the command has on hand. If new or replacement systems are needed, the operations

138                                                          TC 6-0.2                           30 July 2025


---

                                         Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

     SGM brings these requests to the XO. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5202, Develop a Command Post
     Layout.

     1-649. At this point, the commander will have directed which additional CPs are to be established for
     upcoming mission requirements. At the conclusion of Table II, each operations SGM will have a detailed
     configuration of all CP requirements.

DESIGN COMMAND POST CONFIGURATION

     1-650. Commanders validate the layout or design of their CPs during exercises and training events,
     including positioning of personnel, information systems and displays, equipment, network cabling, and
     power supply requirements. Once the operations SGM and XO get approval from the commander on
     their conceptualized plan for each CP, the section NCOICs configure their individual sections based on
     mission requirements. The operations SGM will oversee this action ensuring that system and hardware
     power requirements are being met. From this point through Table IX the operations SGMs will ensure
     layouts are tested, adjusted as required, and captured in the unit’s SOP.

     1-651. Well-designed CPs integrate command and staff efforts. Within a CP, the location of CP cells
     and staff elements are arranged to facilitate internal communication and coordination. Refer to Individual
     Task 150-C2-5202, Develop the Command Post Layout. This arrangement may change over the course
     of operations as the situation changes. Other layout considerations include:

          • Staff integration and crosstalk.
          • Trafficability.
          • COP visibility.
          • Lighting and climate control.
          • Ease and speed of displacement.

REVIEW COMMAND POST STANDARD OPERATING PROCEDURES

     1-652. Section leaders and NCOICs will review and update their section SOPs. These SOPs are nested
     with those of the CP as a whole. Section leaders will ensure that Soldiers review and update SOPs as
     changes to the mission, new equipment fielding, commander’s intent, and orders from higher
     headquarters dictate. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

IDENTIFY FUNCTIONAL GAPS AND COMMERCIAL SOLUTIONS

     1-653. As the design for the CP begins to take shape, and the mission and needs of the commander are
     weighed, the operations SGM will identify parts and equipment that the unit will need to obtain. Refer
     to Individual Task 150-C2-5202, Develop a Command Post Layout.

TABLE III: ESTABLISH COMMAND POST LAYOUT

     1-654. Table III begins with the command receiving a mission, whether it be an upcoming CPX, a CTC
     rotation, or in support of operations. This table begins configuration and functionality of future mission
     requirements into each CP (main, TAC, etc.). This table is performed at home station with the operations
     SGM leading the effort under the direction of the XO. It is understood that CP and C2IS requirements
     are fluid at this point in the tables. However, the operations SGMs and section NCOICs can still get a
     general idea of the infrastructure requirements based on the commander’s decisions in Tables I through
     III. Figure 2-23 on next page establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the
     auditable outputs for the table.

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

     1-655. The XO will inform the operations SGM and section and cell leaders of the mission and related
     IM and KM requirements. These enable the provision of relevant information to the right Soldier at the
     right time and in a usable format in order to facilitate understanding and decision-making, regardless of
     in which CP the Soldier works.

     1-656. Both KM and signal personnel assist the commander and other staff members in developing and
     implementing effective knowledge and information management plans. Detailed doctrine on assessing,
     designing, developing, piloting, and implementing knowledge management solutions is in ATP 6-01.1.
     That ATP also includes doctrine on developing a KMP and offers techniques for content management.
     The operations SGMs will oversee the sections configuring their systems and equipment to conform to
     those information requirements. Refer to Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5330, Conduct Knowledge
     Management, and 71-BDE-5310, Manage Information and Data.

CONDUCT INFORMATION PROTECTION

     1-657. The brigade implements directed information protection measures using operations security
     (OPSEC), information assurance (IA), computer network defense (CND) and electromagnetic protection
     to safeguard and defend friendly information and information systems. The brigade protects information
     regardless of media - telephonic, on paper, on digital devices, or traversing networks and residing on
     information systems - from time collected until the operation is over, the information is archived for
     historical purposes, and the information is authorized to be removed from unit digital and analog
     systems. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5010, Protect Information, and 150-C2-6111, Employ Operations
     Security Control Measures.

INTEGRATE FUNCTIONALITY

     1-658. The majority of staff work occurs within the functional and integrating cells. Staff members must
     also integrate their efforts with other CP cells and staff sections and with organizations external to the
     headquarters. Effective staff integration occurs when functional expertise from across the staff comes
     5346together in support of the commander’s decision making requirements. The operations SGMs will
     lead the section and cell NCOICs to recommend a CP layout. For example, the operations SGM relocates
     the sustainment cell closer to the intelligence cell based on mission enemy reports of enemy forces intent

140                                                 TC 6-0.2                            30 July 2025


---

                                         Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

     to target all logistics routes across the area of operations. The XO then directs a mutual workspace for
     planning. Refer to Individual Task 150-LDR-5251, Organize the Command and Control System.

DETERMINE COMMAND POST ERGONOMICS

     1-659. Ergonomics is defined as people's efficiency in their working environment. Each section leader
     and section NCOIC will ensure that there is adequate working efficiency in the space provided. The
     operations SGM works with sections to help locate, re-locate, or co-locate equipment and personnel that
     may need to be adjusted based on efficiency and the integration functionality mentioned above. Refer to
     Individual Tasks, 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations, and 150-C2-5202, Develop a
     Command Post Layout.

IDENTIFY POWER GENERATION REQUIREMENTS

     1-660. The operations SGM, with the assistance of the section NCOICs, identifies all power
     requirements within the CP. The operations SGM then compares these requirements to the available
     power generation capability, keeping in mind redundancy, fueling, and maintenance requirements of
     systems, and then establishes a CP power generation plan that is both tailorable and portable. The
     operations SGM also ensures operators and mechanics understand their responsibilities with power
     generation. Refer to Individual Task, 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

     1-661. Consult the brigade engineer for subject matter expertise on power generation requirements.

TABLE IV: DETERMINE COMMAND POST SURVIVABILITY
AND SUSTAINABILITY

     1-662. CPs should be set up for a STAFFEX, CPX, or FTX and operational for this table. The
     commander may choose to set up the CP at a particular site and in conjunction with the exercise
     performed by the staff for Table IV, or the commander can direct the table be performed in a motor pool
     setting. For this table, CPs train in isolation to establish internal processes and procedures, with limited
     or simulated interactions between the unit’s CPs and those of higher, adjacent, lower, supporting, and
     supported units. Figure 2-24 on next page establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this
     table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST

     1-663. As part of the Table IV event, the commander directs the establishment of the CP in accordance
     with established unit SOP, the commander’s intent, and operational variables. For example, the brigade
     establishes their main CP at the beginning of a STX event. See Collective Task 71-BDE-0050, Establish
     a Command Post.

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEM
CONSTRUCT

     1-664. As the headquarters prepares for Table V, the commander describes the purposes of the C2IS
     construct for each CP based on mission requirements and the commander’s intent. CP leaders configure
     each CP based on the commander’s guidance, the mission, and current SOPs when refining locations for
     the C2IS within the CP. See Individual Task 150-LDR-5253, Manage Command and Control
     Information Systems.

ESTABLISH THE COMMAND POST SECURITY PLAN

     1-665. With the CP operational, the commander directs the establishment of CP security. The
     headquarters company leadership and the operations SGM, under the guidance of the XO and protection
     cell, review all operational considerations and establish security plans for each CP based on mission
     variables (METT-TC(I)). The team will use Collective Task 19-CO-2204, Conduct Security of Critical
     Sites.

ESTABLISH A CP SUSTAINMENT PLAN

     1-666. With the CPs operational, the headquarters company commander directs the establishment of CP
     sustainment. The operations SGMs, under the guidance of the XO will work with the headquarters
     company supply section and S-4 logistics section to review all operational sustainment considerations

142                                             TC 6-0.2                                              30 July 2025


---

                   Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

     and establish sustainment plans for each CP based on METT-TC(I). The team will refer to the following
     Collective Tasks 71-CO-0433, Support Command Posts.

FINALIZE SHIFT CREWS

     1-667. The operations SGMs, working with the XO, principal staff officers, battle captains, and section
     NCOICs finalize the shifts for each CP based on operational requirements and strengths of the staff.
     Once finalized, the operations SGM and section leads maintain the shifts and allow them to begin the
     process of training as a shift. See Individual Task 150-C2-5205, Lead a Shift Change.

FINALIZE SHIFT CREW RESPONSIBILITIES

     1-668. Once the crews are established, the operations SGMs, with the section NCOICs, will ensure that
     all CP responsibilities are set. For example, all sections know that they will give up one Soldier every
     shift to conduct security. See Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

PREPARE TO PERFORM IN A CONTESTED ELECTROMAGNETIC ENVIRONMENT

     1-669. CP crews must recognize and react to activity in the electromagnetic spectrum (EMS) that effects
     unit use of the EMS. Interference can be deliberately caused by enemy activity, or it can be from friendly
     or civilian uses of the EMS. CPs can also be targeted by the enemy through monitoring a CP’s
     electromagnetic emissions. CP crews and supporting signal Soldiers use Drills 11-CW-D5903 and 11-
     PLT-D5902, both titled React to Electromagnetic Interference, to prepare for this eventuality. CP leaders
     should use GTA 11-11-001, Emission Control Planning Aid, to assist them in preparing the CP for the
     contested EMS.

TABLE V: CONDUCT COMMAND POST REHEARSAL

     1-670. This table is conducted in concert with staff Table V and in accordance with the unit CATS. The
     operations SGM will validate that each CP is operational, meets the commander’s intent, and is ready
     for CP Table VI (Certification). The commander may opt to conduct Tables V and VI with all CPs
     simultaneously and interacting with each other, but the C2TT model does not require this condition until
     Table VII. Figure 2-25 below establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the
     auditable outputs for the table.

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

     1-671. The commander directs the displacement of each CP in accordance with established unit SOP,
     the commander’s intent, and operational variables. Prior to displacing CPs will transfer control of any
     current operations responsibilities to another CP and reassume them once established in the new location.
     See Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5201, Displace the Command Post, 71-BDE-0050, Establish a Command
     Post, 71-BDE-5250, Maintain Continuity of Command and Control.

FINALIZE LOAD PLANS

     1-672. As part of each section understanding their role within the CP, each section leader and section
     NCOIC will ensure that all vehicles and equipment are able to emplace and displace as the mission,
     commander’s intent, and orders from higher dictate. Refer to section SOPs and TMs for section
     equipment. Refer to Collective Task 71-BDE-5200 Conduct Command Post Operations.

FINALIZE COMMAND POST LAYOUT

     1-673. The CP layout is a dynamic, flexible structure that consists of the elements of, and interactions
     between, the OE, the CP, and the C2 system (people, processes, network, and CPs). The operations SGM
     will finalize the CP structure based on feedback from the commander and section leaders, the digital
     architecture in which it resides, and the results of Table V. This includes diagrams of all equipment and
     personnel. 150-C2-5202, Develop a Command Post Layout.

IDENTIFY ELECTROMAGNETIC SIGNATURE

     1-674. Working with the S-6, S-3, protection cell, and cyber personnel, the operations SGM in each CP
     identifies the CPs’ signature. This includes visual, thermal, and electromagnetic signatures. For example,
     the operations SGM identifies the heavy use of push to talk and cell usage within the CP and works with
     the XO and commander to mitigate the risk those devices represent based on threat capabilities. Once
     electromagnetic risks are identified, the operations SGM works to mitigate risks associated with the unit
     digital footprint. Refer to Individual Task 113-25E-3003, Define the Electromagnetic Operations
     Environment.

CONDUCT BATTLE DRILLS

     1-675. Each operations SGM, in conjunction with the battle captain and XO, conducts drills for each
     shift. These drills are mission related and test the functionality of the crews of each shift and should be
     conducted in accordance with the unit SOP. See Collective Task 71-BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post
     Operations. FM 6-0 lists recommended CP battle drills that the brigade develops to meet its mission
     requirements.

     1-676. Upon completion of Table V, the operations SGMs will address all required retraining for their
     CP before moving on to Table VI. The Operations SGM may also use a single event to run the staff and
     CP through Tables III through V for retraining.

SECURE THE SENSITIVE COMPARTMENTALIZED INFORMATION FACILITY [OPTIONAL]

     1-677. Not all brigades have the personnel or equipment to establish SCIF, but for those that do, the SSO
     or S-2 issues guidance for establishing an ACP and implementing physical security measures to exercise
     oversight of a SCIF. The security team leader develops COAs and coordinates operations of attached or
     support elements as required. The security team guards the ACP, implements screening and inspection
     procedures to prevent unauthorized access in accordance with Director of Central Intelligence Directives
     6/9, the SSO’s guidance, and the unit SOP. Refer to Collective Task 34-TM-3091, Establish Access
     Control Point for a Sensitive Compartmented Information Facility (SCIF).

DISPLACE THE COMMAND POST

1-679. Each brigade CP displaces at least once for the CP certification. While displacing a CP, the XO
ensures the primary functions of that CP are handed off to an alternate CP. See Collective Task 71-BDE-
5201, Displace the Command Post.

MAINTAIN CONTINUITY OF COMMAND AND CONTROL

1-680. As part of displacing a CP, the operations SGM will work closely with the XO, battle captains,
and S-6 to ensure that, after C2 has been transferred to the alternate CP, the systems are turned off in a
thought-out progressive manner. The CP will still maintain situational awareness as the CP displaces.
Each of the other unit CPs displace per unit SOP. Refer to Collective Task 71-BDE-5250, Maintain
Continuity of Command and Control.

CERTIFY SHIFT CREWS

1-681. As part of certifying each CP, the commander also certifies the shift crews using Collective Task
71-5485 BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST SECURITY

1-682. The operations SGMs drill CP Soldiers on reacting to security threats to the CP or close to the
CP, and coordinates security requirements for external security with the headquarters company
commander. Refer to Collective Task 19-CO-2204, Conduct Security of Critical Sites. See FM 6-0 for a
list of recommended drills the unit should include in their SOP. Some of those drills apply to CP security
and have been drafted for units to use as the basis for drills in their SOP:

     • 07-BDE-D5147, React to an Unknown Enemy Combatant Within the Perimeter.
     • 07-BDE-D5148, React to Indirect Fire on the Command Post.
     • 07-BDE-D5210, React to Power Outage Within the Command Post.
     • 07-BDE-D7658, React to a Mass Casualty Incident.

SECURE THE SENSITIVE COMPARTMENTALIZED INFORMATION FACILITY [OPTIONAL]

     1-683. Not all brigades have the personnel or equipment to establish a SCIF, but for those that do, the
     SSO or S-2 issues guidance for establishing an ACP and implementing physical security measures to
     exercise oversight of a SCIF. The security team leader develops COAs and coordinates operations of
     attached or support elements as required. The security team guards the ACP, implements screening and
     inspection procedures to prevent unauthorized access in accordance with Director of Central Intelligence
     Directives 6/9, the SSO’s guidance, and the unit SOP. Refer to Collective Task 34-TM-3091, Establish
     Access Control Point for a Sensitive Compartmented Information Facility (SCIF).

     1-684. The outcome of Tables I through VI is that each CP works as a cohesive unit, led by the
     commander, and is ready to proceed to collective training in Table VII with the staff, digital crew, and
     other CPs (the brigade’s own and those of higher, lower, adjacent, supported, and supporting units). The
     CP operates within its designed capabilities and the mission. Individual and section competency meets
     the design of the Army and the commander’s intent. Completing Table VI indicates that the command
     is prepared to conduct CP operations at or above the P level.

     1-685. Upon completion of Table VI and the certification of the CP, the headquarters, led by the XO,
     battle captain, operations SGM, and under the guidance of the commander, transitions to Tables VII
     through X. These tables are a more graduate level of the C2 WFF. In these tables, the CPs integrate
     external elements such as liaisons and unified action partners. The tables lead the command to the CTE
     of the headquarters which is typically an EXEVAL.

TABLE VII: CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-686. Table VII begins with receipt of an order from higher headquarters for the unit’s external
     evaluation. The operations SGMs ensure location and equipment integration. The CPs also execute battle
     drills. The venue and facility location is determined by the commander based on the unit CATS and
     mission requirements. The unit will begin receiving liaisons and building the larger team with
     subordinate units and unified action partners (depending on the unit’s role and mission). Figure 2-27
     below establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the
     table.

                                         Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

INTEGRATE LIAISONS AND ATTACHMENTS

     1-687. As the mission requires and in accordance with unit capabilities, the CP receives liaisons and
     attachments from other units (and any unified action partners in some scenarios). The XO will ensure
     that the additional personnel have the digital and physical requirements in place for connectivity and to
     assist the commander in the conduct of operations. Each WFF cell has a role to play in this process. The
     XO will determine the extent that each cell leader will participate in the reception and integration of
     these liaisons and attachments. If the command receives liaisons or attachments not organic to the unit
     based on the units TO&E/MTO&E, the XO and operations SGM will complete Collective Task 71-
     BDE-5019, Integrate Liaisons and Attachments for Brigade.

     1-688. If multinational partners are participating, key considerations when organizing CPs and
     conducting CP operations in a multinational setting include:

          • Command structure.
          • Staffing.
          • Liaison.
          • Communication.
          • Translators and interpreters.
          • Multinational interoperability.

     1-689. The operations SGM will work with the S-6 and other appropriate representatives to establish
     system and network requirements. The operations SGM and XO reference the multinational
     considerations section in ATP 6-0.5 and FM 3-16 for greater detail.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST OPERATIONS

     1-690. Once the liaisons and attachments are received and integrated, the CPs will conduct CP operations
     in accordance with the commander’s intent, operational requirements, and unit SOP. Refer to Collective
     Task 71-BDE-5200, Conduct Command Post Operations. The TAC CP or other CPs will meet the same
     criteria and Table VII-X standards as the unit main CP. For example, the brigade TAC CP will conduct
     battle drills in accordance with Table VII standards.

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

     1-691. The XO will inform the operations SGM and section and cell leaders of the mission and related
     IM and KM requirements. Combined KM and IM enables the provision of relevant information to the
     right person at the right time and in a usable format in order to facilitate understanding and decision-
     making. All staff sections and CPs contribute to developing the KMP.

     1-692. The CP personnel assist the commander and other staff members in implementing effective KM
     and IM plans. ATP 6-01.1 includes doctrine on developing a KMP and offers techniques for content
     management. The operations SGM will oversee the sections configuring their systems and equipment to
     conform to those information requirements. Refer to Collective Tasks 71-BDE-5330, Conduct
     Knowledge Management, and 71-BDE-5310, Manage Information and Data.

CONDUCT COMMAND POST BATTLE DRILLS

     1-693. As part of conducting the scenario driven by a collective tactical task, the XO or OIC of each CP
     will initiate CP battle drills. The operations SGM will use any of the applicable following doctrinal drills
     to develop and rehearse battle drills for the brigade SOP:

          • Drill 07-BDE-D5315, React to Incorrect Information Affecting the Operational Environment.
          • Drill 07-BDE-D5210, React to Power Outage Within the Command Post.
          • Drill 71-BDE-D6320, React to a Degraded Network.
          • Drill 71-BDE-D7658, React to a Mass Casualty Incident.
          • Drill 71-BDE-D7660, React to Civil Disturbance.

     to and from the CP schedules. Refer to Individual Task 150-C2-5200, Conduct Command Post

     for the external evaluation / validation exercise. The intent is that this exercise match as closely as
     possible the training circumstances and requirements anticipated for the Table X, The Command and

     to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

SECTION V – BRIGADE DIGITAL CREW TRAINING TABLES

     1-719. Section V discusses the command and control training tables for the brigade digital crew.

COMMAND AND CONTROL TRAINING TABLES FOR THE
BRIGADE DIGITAL CREW

     1-720. This chapter is designed to enable the commander and staff to train and evaluate the ability of
     staff and C2IS operators to integrate systems and generate the COP. The COP is a single display of
     relevant information tailored to unit requirements that simultaneously creates shared understanding
     horizontally and vertically across an organization. This shared understanding is measured by the ability
     of commanders and their staffs to make timely decisions.

     1-721. Key trainers for the digital crew are the C2 information integrators (Soldiers expert in integrating
     C2 systems to establish the COP that lead digital crews), battle captains, KMOs, signal DMGs, and staff
     section leaders. It is also recommended that a trained C2 information integrator be assigned to every CP
     or node.

     1-722. The digital crew consists of the unit C2 information integrators, the COP managers, and the
     individual C2IS operators in all shifts of all CPs. The tables for digital crew assume the individual C2IS
     operators are certified to operate their C2IS according to the appropriate proponent manuals. The C2
     information integrator prepares the digital crew training and the certification program with the following
     Individual Tasks: 150-DMG-2030, Coordinate Command and Control Information Systems with
     Enablers, 150-DMG-2031 Develop a Digital Training Program (Unit, Individual, or Crew), and 150-
     DMG-2032, Execute the Digital Crew Training Program. The C2 information integrator is focused on
     the integration of C2IS and is not trained to be the operator or expert of each C2IS. Execution of training
     tables occurs concurrently with other planned training to support organizational training requirements.

30 July 2025  TC 6-0.2  153


---

Chapter 2

     1-723. The digital crew integrates C2 information systems to display information that enables the
     commander in the decision-making process. Led by the C2 information integrator, the digital crew is
     responsible for advising the staff on the most effective techniques to integrate their C2IS. The digital
     crews coordinate with the COP manager to integrate critical information from each WFF into the COP
     to support the commander’s visualization.

     1-724. The composition of the digital crew at the brigade echelon is adjusted to support commander’s
     guidance. Considerations for guidance include training, education, experience of available personnel,
     and their ability to integrate their C2IS. The C2 information integrator and COP manager recommend
     composition and disposition of C2IS to the XO tailored to the OE. Planning factors include shift
     changeover, relocation of the CP, requirements for split operations, and mobile CP operations.

     1-725. The S-3 is responsible for the training and management of the digital crew to produce the unit’s
     COP through the execution of the digital crew training tables. The digital crew executes the training
     tables to support the commander’s guidance and training objectives under the supervision of the
     operations officer with support from the C2 information integrator and signal officer. Considerations for
     training include stability and proficiency of digital crews, available equipment, training calendar, and
     operational need. The digital training tables are designed to be executed sequentially as each table builds
     on skills learned in previous tables. Tables I through VI focus on the digital crew building proficiency
     with some requirements from the staff (e.g., graphics developed during MDMP). The S-3 should verify
     at the end of Table V training that the digital crew is prepared for the commander’s validation in Table
     VI. Tables VII through X focus on the employment and integration of the staff supported by the digital
     crews.

     1-726. Digital crews are rated as fully qualified, qualified, or unqualified with a maximum and minimum
     performance standard within each rating. The intent is for the digital crew to participate in the tables
     simultaneously as an organization.

          • Fully qualified – All assigned crew members have successfully completed Tables I-VI; at
                minimum, every member of the digital crew has completed these tables.

          • Qualified – 80% of the digital crew have successfully collectively completed Tables III-VI; at
                minimum, every member of the digital crew has completed Tables I and II.

          • Unqualified – 1 or more crew members have not completed Tables I-II or more than 20% of
                the digital crewmembers in the organization’s headquarters have not completed Tables III-VI.

     1-727. Once a crew is fully qualified, commanders are advised to maintain digital crew integrity, similar
     to battle-rostering weapons systems. When replacing crew members, the new crew member must be
     individually qualified on Tables I and II for the crew to maintain a qualified rating. If more than 20% of
     crew members are replaced from the original fully qualified crew, the digital crew is considered
     unqualified until the new crew conducts Tables III through VI as a collective unit.

     and the auditable outputs for the table.

SET UP THE COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEM FOR OPERATIONS

    1-729. Given the variety of WFF information systems in the Army and version updates associated with
    each, the C2 information integrator will ensure that Soldiers of the digital crew are operating on their
    MTO&E authorized systems in accordance with associated TMs and most recently approved software.
    The commander directs which systems and configuration to use based on mission requirements, the OE
    (current and forecasted), unit capabilities, equipment/systems and versions through Army fielding
    initiatives, and higher headquarters requirements. The C2 information integrator determines what tasks
    are selected from the TM to validate the operators’ abilities to employ the C2IS. Of note, the C2
    information integrator should consider tasks based on the operator’s duties / responsibilities and unit
    SOP. Individual Task 150-C2-5250, Employ a Command and Control Information System, and system
    TMs suggest tasks the operators should complete for this verification which should include:

         • Set up the C2IS for operation (Prepare the C2IS for Operations).
         • Employ the C2IS for operations.
         • Organize C2IS data.
         • Operate C2IS degraded (150-DMG-2020, Exercise Command and Control Under Degraded

              Conditions).

    1-730. The desired outcome for Table I is for the digital crew to understand the commander’s guidance
    for employment of C2IS and demonstrate proficiency on the respective systems. Additionally, the digital
    crew must be able to sustain operations throughout degraded conditions and understand use of analog
    tracking systems.

TABLE II: INTEGRATE COMMAND POST SYSTEMS

    1-731. The purpose of this table is to ensure that all digital crew members understand the integration of
    their individual systems into the network and can effectively communicate across the network. Execution
    validates the C2IS integration across the staff as outlined in KM SOPs to include setting permissions
    and sharing data. This table typically requires collaboration of the ‘digital triad’ (the C2 information
    integrator, signal digital master gunner (signal officer for units without the signal DMG), and KMO) in
    addition to appointed COP manager, S-3, and S-6. The appointed COP manager assists in the
    management of information for use in the COP. Digital crew members must be proficient at sharing
    information through the entire network at the conclusion of this table. If the entire network is not
    available or utilized for the table, the S-3, S-6, and C2 information integrator determine how to mitigate
    the associated risk and communicate their approach to the commander. Figure 2-32 on next page
    establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

INTEGRATE C2 INFORMATION SYSTEMS WITH THE MISSION COMMAND WORKSTATION

     1-732. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2033, Integrate C2
     Information Systems (C2IS) with the Mission Command Workstation (MCWS). Execution of this task
     allows the C2IS operator associated with each warfighting function to share information through the
     MCWS to update the COP. The C2 information integrator will ensure that each operator can publish
     graphics in accordance with unit SOPs for dissemination to all authorized users. The C2 information
     integrator also evaluates the C2IS operator’s ability to import and export graphics. The operations SGM,
     battle captain, or battle NCO along with the C2 information integrator will confirm the digital crew’s
     ability to import and share messages, reports, and overlays.

PERFORM TROUBLESHOOTING PROCEDURES ON C2IS

     1-733. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-3003, Perform
     Troubleshooting Procedures on the Command and Control Information System. Operators use
     equipment and software TMs and users guides to troubleshoot problems with C2IS hardware or software.
     System operators will identify and correct system failure with successful functions check or escalate to
     higher level support in accordance with the applicable TMs. When this task is complete, the workstation
     is fully mission capable, or deficiencies and shortcomings are annotated and required maintenance
     actions to be performed by someone other than the workstation's operator are identified. The C2
     information integrator and COP manager will observe and evaluate the performance measures of this
     task with the battle captain who will ensure that updates are provided in accordance with the digital SOP.

TABLE III: DEVELOP THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

     1-734. Table III focuses on the development of the COP through collaboration of the digital crew, the
     COP manager, and staff operating within the commander’s guidance. Each member of the digital crew
     will publish their respective digital efforts (graphics) through the network to populate the commander’s
     COP, providing real-time situational awareness. The commander tailors the COP through guidance to
     the staff WFF representatives who provide feedback to ensure achievement of commander’s intent. The
     digital crew trains to support the commander’s and staff’s situational awareness through digitizing
     relevant information using the C2IS to display the digital COP. The updated COP reflects the most
     current events tailored to CCIR to support the commander’s decision-making process. Figure 2-33 on
     next page establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for
     the table.

ESTABLISH THE COMMON OPERATIONAL PICTURE

1-735. The C2 information integrator and COP manager will use Individual Task 150-C2-5315,
Establish the Common Operational Picture. This task is critical to the success of the staff and digital
crew and may require coordination with the COP manager, battle captain, or S-3 to validate the digital
crew’s ability to produce the COP. The COP manager and battle captain update the COP based on the
mission, relevant information imported from C2IS, and information from other sources to enable the
commander’s decisions making.

PREPARE THE DIGITAL COP

1-736. The C2 information integrator leads the digital crew to develop a list of items to display on the
COP, and practice putting them in appropriate C2IS, including:

     • Boundaries.
     • Phase lines.
     • Objectives.
     • Maneuver graphics.
     • Fire support coordination measures (FSCM).
     • Airspace coordinating measures (ACM).
     • Known and suspected enemy locations.
     • Supply routes and depots.
     • Decision points.

1-737. The digital crew will determine which elements of the COP will be automatically populated on
the COP versus those the crew will have to enter manually and practice doing so.

1-738. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2035, Prepare the Digital
Common Operational Picture, to guide the crew through Table III.

IMPLEMENT THE KNOWLEDGE MANAGEMENT PLAN

1-739. The KMO will inform the operations SGM, C2 information integrator, and section and cell
leaders of the mission of mission-related information and KM requirements. The COP, as part of the
unit’s combined KM and IM plans, enables the provision of relevant information to the right person at
the right time and in a usable format in order to facilitate understanding and decision-making. The C2
information integrator supervises the digital crew in establishing this key component of the KMP. The
C2 information integrator participates in developing a KMP and digital portions of the unit SOP. The
C2 information integrator will oversee the sections configuring their systems and equipment to conform

EXECUTE BATTLE DRILLS

     1-742. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2015, Execute Battle Drills
     Using Command and Control Information Systems. The successful unit will have an established list of
     approved battle drills that are published in the appropriate unit SOP (such as CP, plans, and digital). The
     staff reviews and executes battle drills in accordance with SOP and commander’s guidance for training.
     This process is supervised by the COP manager, operations SGM, or S-3. The battle captain determines
     if execution of the battle drills achieves commander’s information requirements outlined in the SOP.
     The staff verifies that the battle drills support their WFF through screening criteria, setting conditions
     for operational success.

SHARE A PLAN USING COMMAND AND CONTROL INFORMATION SYSTEMS

     1-743. The C2 information integrator will use Individual Task 150-DMG-3006, Share a Plan Using the
     Command and Control Information Systems. The staff assists the commander’s visualization through
     use of C2IS visualization aides including maps, charts, and tables. The C2 information integrator assists
     the COP manager, battle captain, or S-3 to select a briefing format that supports the commander’s
     guidance and mission requirements.

PRESENT A COMMAND POST UPDATE BRIEFING

     1-744. The C2 information integrator and COP manager will use Individual Task 150-DMG-2016,
     Present Command Post Update Briefing. The CP update brief is the culmination of the integration and
     information sharing to create situational and shared understanding. The battle captain, S-3, or
     commander validates the update briefing to ensure it meets the commander’s guidance. A briefing
     manager is designated by the XO or S-3 to organize and control the briefing (typically the battle captain
     or battle NCO) in each CP. The designated briefing manager guides staff preparation for the update in
     coordination with the COP manager and C2 information integrator. Briefing preparation techniques
     traditionally focus on gaining efficiency by leveraging C2IS capabilities throughout the presentation.
     Briefing concludes with a rehearsal by WFF.

TABLE V: CONDUCT COMMAND AND CONTROL NETWORK
REHEARSAL

     1-745. The network rehearsal presents the last opportunity in digital training tables for commanders to
     refine their processes before commanders certify their digital crews in Table VI. Table V is scenario
     driven and evaluates the digital crew’s ability to use digital systems to support the operations process.
     Retraining may be required through multiple scenarios to ensure compliance with battle drills and refine
     unit SOPs. At the conclusion of Table V the digital crew should be operating at full capacity.

     1-746. Once the digital crew successfully completes all requirements of Table V, the C2 information
     integrator reports to the S-3, the operations SGM, and each battle captain that the digital crew is prepared
     for the certification event (Table VI). Figure 2-35 below establishes the inputs, tasks to be accomplished

ESTABLISH THE COP

     1-751. The COP manager will use Collective Task 71-BDE-5319, Establish a Common Operational
     Picture. This table is executed during events that require the headquarters staff to enable the
     commander’s validation of the digital crew for the establishment of the COP and execution of an update
     brief. The digital crews will use SOPs established during Tables I through V.

     1-752. Upon successful completion of Table VI, the brigade commander certifies the digital crews. Any
     crew considered qualified or above is proficient at system integration, COP development, and
     synchronizing operations. They is able to provide integration advice to the staff during Tables VII
     through X and fully integrate their systems into the unit’s operations process.

     1-753. Digital crew members will use their C2IS to battle track, support the staff, execute battle drills,
     maintain running estimates, conduct briefings, and manage the commander's information requirements

160                                             TC 6-0.2                                                30 July 2025

                                  Command and Control Warfighting Function Training Tables – Brigades

     1-754. The outcome of Tables I through VI is that digital crews now operate as a cohesive unit. Each
     system operator understands and competently performs in their warfighting function staff role to support
     the mission. Completing Table VI indicates that the digital crew is prepared to support the command in
     the conduct of LSCO. Upon completion of Table VI and the certification of the C2IS, the digital crew,
     led by the C2 information integrator and COP manager, transitions to Tables VII through X.

     1-755. Tables VII through X are designed to train the staff and digital crew as an integrated team. The
     certified digital crew will assist and enhance the staff’s ability to conduct the operations process. As the
     unit transitions into Tables VII through X, the C2 information integrator also transitions from controlling
     the certification process to serving as an advisor to the command team. The C2 information integrator
     now serves as the subject matter expert on digital training and integration.

     1-756. Digital crews must be familiar with all aspects of their digital systems and understand how they
     add value to the commander and staff through their C2IS. While the digital crew is considered an integral
     part of the staff for Tables VII through X, the digital crew’s proficiency and actions are evaluated
     separately from the staff sections. This will allow the commander to validate the digital crew’s
     qualification ratings separately from the staff to meet training objectives or for retraining purposes, and
     to identify and isolate problems should the arise.

     1-757. The commander uses the tables VII-IX as a means of integrating the digital crew and the systems
     into the headquarters and staff functions to obtain a higher level of proficiency. The C2 information
     integrator and digital triad works with the staff to refine the digital SOP and develop a retraining plan to
     ensure that the digital crew members maintain proficiency and operate effectively and efficiently within
     their staff section.

     1-758. Upon completion of certifications in Table VI, the unit will receive an order from the headquarters
     responsible for the brigade EXEVAL in Table X. The digital crew will use the information from this
     order to train tables VII through IX.

TABLE VII: INTEGRATE THE COMMAND AND CONTROL
SYSTEM

     1-759. Table VII is led by the C2 information integrator operating under commander, XO, and S-3
     guidance to integrate additional C2IS and their operators brought to the unit by liaisons and attachments
     into the digital crew as needed. It begins with the establishment of the CP and digital systems to include
     staff coordination with horizontal and vertical elements. This table includes establishing the network,
     C2 system integration, and establishing and disseminating the COP. Figure 2-37 below establishes the
     inputs, tasks to be accomplished during this table, and the auditable outputs for the table.

ESTABLISH A COMMAND POST

     1-760. The XO will direct the S-3 and the operations SGM to prepare to establish the CPs per unit SOP.
